<sig>G 2</sig>

However, the characters that are derived <note>original: "extracted"</note> from the name of a spirit are constructed using the following table, by assigning each letter the corresponding name from that table. While this may seem easy to those who understand it, a significant challenge arises in knowing which figure or letter to choose when a letter from the name matches either the row of letters or the row of figures. 

This is determined as follows: If a letter lands on the row of letters, consider its position within the name (for example, whether it is the second or third letter); then, consider how many letters the entire name contains (such as five or seven). Multiply these two numbers together and triple the result. Then, count that total number through the alphabet, starting from the beginning. The letter you land on should be used as the character for that spirit. 

But if any letter of a name lands on the row of figures, it should be calculated this way: Take the letter’s position in the name and multiply it by its numerical position in the alphabet. Take the result <note>original: "and being added together"</note> and divide it by nine. The remainder indicates the figure or number to be used in the character. This can be represented as either a geometric shape or an arithmetic numeral, though it should not exceed the number nine (referring to the nine orders of angels <note>In occult numerology, the numbers 1 through 9 often correspond to the nine hierarchies of angels.</note>).

But the characters that are understood through the revelation of spirits derive their power <note>original: "vertue"</note> from that source; because they are, as it were, certain hidden seals representing the harmony of some divinity. They are either signs of a covenant entered into, or of promised and pledged loyalty, or of obedience. These characters cannot be discovered by any other means.

Moreover, besides these characters, there are certain familiar figures and images of evil spirits, the forms in which they are accustomed to appear and yield obedience to those who call upon <note>original: "invoke"</note> them. All these characters or images may be seen using the table that follows, according to the sequence of the letters that make up the names of the spirits themselves. If more than one spirit’s name is found in any single letter, the image of the first takes priority <note>original: "holdeth the pre-eminence"</note>, while the others contribute their own traits. To those spirits who belong to the first orders, the head and the upper part of the body are assigned according to their own figure. Those which are the lowest possess the thighs and feet. Likewise, the middle letters assign the middle parts of the body to create a fitting form. But if any conflict <note>original: "contrariety"</note> occurs, the letter that is stronger in number shall rule; if they are equal, they will contribute equal parts. Furthermore, if any name derives any notable character or instrument from the Table, that spirit shall likewise have that same symbol in its image.

We may also gain knowledge of the ranks <note>original: "dignities"</note> of the evil spirits by the same tables of characters and images; for whichever spirit is assigned any excellent sign or instrument from the Table of Characters, he possesses that rank. For example, if there is a crown, it shows a royal rank; if a crest or plume, a dukedom; if a horn, the rank of a count <note>original: "a County"</note>; if with—

If among these there is a scepter, sword, or forked instrument, it shows rule and authority. Likewise, from the Table of Images, you will find those who hold the highest kingly dignity. Judge their dignity by the crown, and their rule and authority by the instruments they carry. Finally, those who take a human shape and figure have greater dignity than those who appear in the forms and images of beasts; likewise, those who ride excel those who appear on foot. In this way, according to all their various combinations <note>original: "commixtures"</note>, you may judge the rank and excellence of spirits relative to one another. Furthermore, you must understand that spirits of a lower order, regardless of their rank, are always subject to spirits of a higher order. Thus, it is not inconsistent <note>original: "incongruent"</note> for their kings and dukes to be subject to and serve the presiding spirits of the higher order.

## *The shapes familiar to the Spirits of Saturn.*

**T**hey appear for the most part with a tall, lean, and slender body and an angry facial expression <note>original: "countenance"</note>. Their movement is like the rushing of the wind, accompanied by a kind of earthquake. Their sign is white earth, whiter than any snow.

## The typical forms of the Spirits of Jupiter.

->♃<-
<note>This is the astronomical and alchemical symbol for the planet Jupiter.</note>

The Spirits of Jupiter appear with a body that is sanguine and choleric <note>In the medical theory of the time, "sanguine" suggested a ruddy, energetic nature, while "choleric" suggested a passionate or quick-tempered disposition.</note>, of medium height <note>original: "middle stature"</note>, with a movement that is horrible and fearful. However, they have a mild countenance <note>original: "countenance": facial expression</note>, gentle speech, and are the color of iron. Their movement is accompanied by flashes of lightning and thunder; their sign is that men will appear around the ritual circle who seem to be devoured by lions.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;But we now come to speak of the holy and sacred pentacles <note>original: "Pentacles": amulets or talismans used in magical rituals, often inscribed with symbols.</note> and sigils <note>original: "Sigils": symbols or seals representing a specific spirit or magical intention.</note>. These pentacles are, as it were, certain holy signs protecting us from evil chances and events, and helping and assisting us to bind, banish, and drive away evil spirits, while attracting good spirits and reconciling them to us. 

And these pentacles consist either of the symbols <note>original: "Characters": in this context, specific occult signs or written marks associated with spirits.</note> of the good spirits of the superior order, or of sacred pictures of holy letters or revelations, with appropriate verses. These are composed either of geometrical figures and holy names of God—according to the tradition and manner of many of them—or they are compounded of all of these, or many of them mixed together. 

The symbols that are useful for us to establish and make the pentacles are the signs of the good spirits—especially and chiefly those of the good spirits of the first and second order, and sometimes also of the third order <note>Referring to the traditional hierarchies of angels and celestial beings.</note>. This kind of symbol is especially to be called holy, along with those symbols which we have previously referred to as holy. 

Therefore, whatever symbol of this kind is to be established, we must draw a double circle around it, in which we must write the name of its angel. If we wish to add some divine name compatible with that spirit and its office, it will have even greater power and effectiveness. And if we wish to draw any angular figure around it according to the logic of its numbers <note>This refers to numerology and the "magic squares" (Kameas) associated with the planets.</note>, that also is permitted to be done. But as for the holy pictures that make up the pentacles,

# *Of Occult Philosophy*

<margin>The brazen serpent set up in the wilderness <note>A reference to the biblical story in Numbers 21, where Moses set up a bronze serpent on a pole to heal the Israelites.</note>.</margin>
These are those that are delivered to us everywhere in the Prophets and sacred writings, in both the Old and New Testaments. Examples include the figure of the serpent hanging on the cross and similar symbols; many of these may be found in the visions of the Prophets—such as Isaiah <note>original: "Efaias"</note>, Daniel, Ezra <note>original: "Esdras"</note>, and others—and also in the book of Revelation <note>original: "Apocalypse"</note>. We have spoken of them in our third book of *Occult Philosophy*, where we mentioned holy things. 

Therefore, when a picture of any of these holy images is positioned, let a circle be drawn around it on all sides. Inside that circle, let there be written some divine name that is appropriate and matches the intended effect of that figure. Alternatively, one may write a short verse <note>original: "versicle"</note> taken from the Holy Scriptures around it, which may serve to secure or pray for the desired effect. 

For instance, if a <term>pentacle</term> <note>A magical talisman or symbol, often inscribed with names or figures, used for protection or to command spirits.</note> were to be made to gain victory or revenge against one's enemies—both visible and invisible—the figure may be taken from the second book of the Maccabees. 

<margin>Take the holy sword, a gift from God, with which you shall strike down the adversaries of my people Israel. <note>original: "Accipe gladium sanctum, mumus [munus] a Deo, in quo concides adversarios populi mei Israel."</note></margin>
That is to say, the image of a hand holding a drawn golden sword, around which should be written the verse found there: *Take the holy sword, the gift of God, with which you shall slay the adversaries of my people Israel.* Or, one might write a verse from the fifth Psalm: *In this is the strength of your arm; before your face there is death*; or some other similar verse. 

But if you choose to write a divine name around the figure, then select a name that signifies Fear, a Sword, Wrath, the Vengeance of God, or some other name that aligns with the desired effect. If an angular figure <note>A geometric shape such as a triangle or square used in occult diagrams.</note> is to be drawn, let it be chosen according to the logic and rules of numbers, as we taught in our second book of *Occult Philosophy*, where we treated the subject of numbers and similar operations. 

Of this sort, there are two <term>pentacles</term> of sublime virtue and great power, which are very useful and necessary for the consecration of experiments and spirits. One of these is the one described in the first chapter of Revelation: the figure of the Majesty of God sitting upon a throne, having in his mouth a two-edged sword, as is written there. Around this, let there be...

figure; or by any number which may be divided without any surplus or decrease. Now once such a name is found, whether it is only one name or more, or several names, it should be written in all the various angles of the figure. But in the middle of the figure, let the <term>revolution</term> <note>A magical arrangement of letters, often in a circle or square, where the letters of a name are shifted or rotated to create a new pattern.</note> of the name be placed in its entirety, or at least its primary parts.

Often we also create <term>pentacles</term> <note>Holy talismans or diagrams inscribed with magical names and symbols used for protection or power.</note> by arranging the revolution of some kind of name in a square table, drawing a single or double circle around it, and writing within it some holy verse <note>original: "versicle"</note> that is appropriate and fits this name, or from which that name was originally derived. And this is the method for making pentacles according to their various distinct forms and styles. We may multiply or mix these together as we please to achieve greater effectiveness and to extend and enlarge their power and virtue.

For example, if a prayer <note>original: "deprecation"; in this context, a prayer intended to avert evil or invoke judgment upon an enemy</note> is made for the overthrow and destruction of one’s enemies, then we should remember and call to mind how God destroyed the entire face of the earth in the Great Flood; and the destruction of Sodom and Gomorrah by raining down fire and sulfur <note>original: "brimstone"</note>. Likewise, remember how God overthrew *Pharaoh* and his army in the Red Sea, and recall any other curse <note>original: "malediction"</note> found in Holy Scripture <note>original: "Writ"</note>. And so it is with similar matters.

Likewise, when praying against the perils and dangers of water, we should recall the saving of *Noah* in the Great Flood, the passage of the children of *Israel* through the Red Sea; and also we are to remember how Christ walked upon the water and saved the ship that was in danger of being wrecked by the storm; and how he commanded the winds and the waves and they obeyed him; and also that he pulled *Peter* out of the water when he was in danger of drowning, and other such instances. 

Finally, with these, we invoke and call upon certain holy names of God—specifically those that are significant for accomplishing our desire and suited to the intended effect. For instance, if the goal is to overthrow enemies, we should invoke and call upon the names of God associated with wrath, revenge, fear, justice, and strength <note>original: "fortitude"</note>. If we wish to avoid and escape any evil or danger, we then call upon

### The Fourth Book

The names of Mercy, Defense, Salvation, Fortitude, Goodness, and similar names of God should be used. When we pray to God to grant our desires, we should also mix in the name of some good spirit—whether just one or several—whose office <note>A spirit's "office" refers to its specific duty, role, or area of influence in the celestial hierarchy.</note> it is to carry out our desires. Sometimes we also require an evil spirit to stop or to compel someone, and we intermingle their name as well. This is done rightly, especially if the goal is to carry out an act of justice such as revenge, punishment, or destruction.

Furthermore, if there is any verse <note>original: "versicle"</note> in the Psalms or any other part of Holy Scripture that seems relevant and agreeable to our desire, it should be mingled with our prayers. After a prayer has been made to God, it is then appropriate to deliver an oration to that "executioner" <note>In this context, an "executioner" is the being—angel, spirit, or celestial body—assigned to carry out the request.</note> whom we requested in our previous prayer to serve us. This might be one or more beings, whether an angel, a star, a soul, or any of the noble angels. This kind of oration ought to be composed according to the rules we provided in the second book of *Occult Philosophy*, where we treated the method for composing incantations.

<margin>* Fires; Envy and Malice. <note>original: "Incendia"</note></margin>
<margin>* "You shall walk upon the asp and the basilisk, etc." <note>original Latin: "Super aspidem & basiliscum Ambulabis, &c." This is a reference to Psalm 91:13.</note></margin>

You should also know that these kinds of "bonds" <note>Magical constraints or obligations used to command spirits or forces of nature.</note> have three distinct types. The first bond is when we conjure by natural things. The second is composed of religious mysteries—using sacraments, miracles, and things of that sort. The third is established by divine names and holy <term>sigils</term> <note>Magical symbols or seals representing a specific deity or spirit.</note>. By these kinds of bonds, we may bind not only spirits but also any other creatures whatsoever, such as animals, tempests, *fires*, floods, and the force and power of weapons.

Often we use these aforementioned bonds not only through conjuration <note>Formal summoning or commanding.</note> but sometimes also through <term>deprecation</term> <note>A prayer intended to avert evil or invoke a curse.</note> and benediction <note>A blessing.</note>. Moreover, it is very helpful for this purpose to join some sentence of Holy Scripture, if any can be found that is suitable. For example, in the conjuration of serpents, one might commemorate the curse of the serpent in the earthly Paradise and the raising of the bronze serpent in the wilderness, further adding that verse: *You shall walk upon the asp and the basilisk, etc.*

# Of Occult Philosophy,

<page-num>50</page-num>

link, etc. Superstition is also very prevalent in this area, specifically through the adaptation <note>original: "translation"</note> of certain sacramental rites to bind that which we intend to hinder; such as the rites of excommunication, burials <note>original: "sepulchres"</note>, funerals, interments, and the like.

And now we come to treat of the consecrations which a practitioner ought to perform upon all instruments and things necessary to be used in this art. The virtue of this consecration primarily consists in two things: namely, in the power of the person performing the consecration, and by the virtue of the prayer by which the consecration is made. 

Regarding the person performing the consecration, holiness of life and the power of sanctifying are required. Both of these are acquired through dignification <note>The process of being made worthy or elevated in spiritual status through ritual and merit.</note> and initiation. Furthermore, the person themselves must believe with a firm and undoubted faith in the virtue, power, and efficacy of the act. 

Then, regarding the prayer itself by which this consecration is made, a similar holiness is required. This holiness either consists solely in the prayer itself—for instance, if it is ordained for this purpose by divine inspiration, such as those found in many places in the Holy Bible—or it is instituted for this purpose through the power of the Holy Spirit in the rites <note>original: "ordination"</note> of the Church. Otherwise, there is a sanctity <note>original: "sanctimony"</note> in the prayer that comes not only from itself, but from the commemoration of holy things. This includes the mention of Holy Scriptures, histories, works, miracles, effects, graces, promises, sacraments, sacramental items, and the like. These things, through a certain similarity, seem to properly or metaphorically <note>original: "improperly"</note> belong to the thing being consecrated.

The invocation of certain divine names is also used, along with the marking <note>original: "consignation"</note> of holy seals and similar actions that lead to sanctification and purification <note>original: "expiation"</note>. These include sprinkling with holy water, anointing with holy oil, and fragrant incense <note>original: "odoriferous suffumigations"</note> belonging to holy worship. Therefore, in every consecration, the blessing and consecration of water, oil, fire, and incense <note>original: "fumigations"</note> are primarily used, and these are always used with holy wax candles or burning lamps; for no sacrament is properly performed without lights. 

This, therefore, is to be known and firmly observed: if any consecration is to be made of secular <note>original: "profane"; meaning common or non-sacred items.</note> things in which there is any...

But if we wish to summon any evil spirit to a *Circle*, it is first necessary for us to consider and know its nature—which of the *Planets* it corresponds to, and what duties are assigned to it by that *Planet*. Once this is known, a place should be sought out that is fit and proper for its summoning, according to the nature of the *Planet* and the quality of the spirit's duties, as nearly as possible. For example, if their power is over the *Sea, Rivers*, or *Floods*, let the place be chosen on the *Shore*; and so on for the rest.

Then, a convenient time should be chosen, both regarding the quality of the *Air*—which should be serene, clear, quiet, and fitting for spirits to take on physical bodies—as well as the quality and nature of the *Planet* and the *Spirit*. This means summoning them on their designated day or during the time they rule. This timing may be fortunate or unfortunate, occurring sometimes during the day and sometimes during the night, as the *Stars* and *Spirits* require. Once you have obtained what you desire from the Spirit, or are otherwise satisfied, grant him leave <note>original: "license him"</note> to depart with courteous words, commanding him to do no harm. Then, once all these things have been performed in an orderly manner, you may depart.

But those spirits that obstinately refuse to yield to these things are to be compelled with threats, <term>comminations</term> <note>original: "Comminations"; formal denunciations or threats of divine punishment</note>, curses, delusions, and <term>contumelies</term> <note>original: "Contumelies"; insulting or humiliating language</note>, and especially by threatening to expel them from the places they frequent.

Lastly, when you wish to invoke these kinds of spirits, you should prepare a table in the place of invocation covered with clean linen. Upon this, you shall set fresh bread and running water or milk in new earthen vessels, along with new knives. You shall also make a fire, upon which a perfume shall be offered. Let the invoker go to the head of the table, and around it let seats be placed for the spirits as you see fit; once the spirits are called, you shall invite them to drink and eat. But if you happen to fear any evil spirit, then draw a circle around the table, and let the part of the table where the invoker sits be within the circle, and the rest of the table be outside the circle.

In our third book of *Occult Philosophy*, we have taught how and by what means the soul is joined to the body, and what happens to the soul after death.

You should also know that those souls still love their abandoned bodies after death, as if attracted by a certain affinity; such are the souls of <term>noxious</term> <note>original: "noxious"; here meaning wicked, harmful, or criminal</note> men who have violently left their bodies, and souls lacking a proper burial, which still wander in a liquid and turbulent <meta>catchword: bulent; completed from the start of the next page</meta>...

<summary>This page provides practical instructions for invoking nature spirits through offerings of food and music, while also beginning a discussion on why certain souls—specifically the wicked or unburied—remain tethered to the physical world after death.</summary>
<keywords>nature spirits, ritual offerings, exorcism, magic circle, soul after death, Agrippa, Occult Philosophy, necromancy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of spirits, moving specifically into the practice of necromancy—the conjuring of the souls of the deceased—and the physical materials and locations required for such rituals.</meta>

<page-num>67</page-num>
<header>*The Fourth Book*</header>

...turbulent spirits about their dead carcasses; for these souls, by the known means by which they were previously joined to their bodies—by similar vapors, liquids, and scents—are easily drawn to them.

<margin>Necromancy practices, pages 70, 71.</margin>
Because of this, the souls of the dead are not to be called up without blood, or by the application of some part of their <term>relict body</term> <note>original: "relict Body"; the remains or corpse left behind after death</note>.

In the raising up of these <term>shadows</term> <note>original: "shadows"; from the Latin *umbrae*, referring to the spirits or ghosts of the deceased</note>, we must burn perfumes made with fresh blood, the bones of the dead, and with flesh, eggs, milk, honey, and oil, and similar things. These provide the souls with a means suitable for receiving their bodies.

It should also be understood that those who desire to raise any souls of the dead ought to do so in those places where these kinds of souls are most known to linger. This might be due to some alliance drawing those souls back to their forsaken bodies, or because of some past affection impressed upon them during life that draws the souls to certain places, things, or people. Alternatively, they may be drawn by the powerful nature of a place designed to purge or punish these souls. These locations are mostly identified through the experience of visions, nightly hauntings, apparitions, and similar <term>prodigies</term> <note>original: "prodigies"; extraordinary or supernatural events seen as omens or signs</note> observed there.

Therefore, the most fitting places for these rituals are churchyards. Even better than those are places where criminal sentences have been carried out. And better still are places where public massacres have occurred in recent years. Furthermore, a place is even better if a corpse resulting from a violent death remains there—one that has not yet been <term>expiated</term> <note>original: "expiated"; purified through religious ritual to atone for the violence of the death</note> or properly buried, especially if it was buried recently. The purification of these places is a holy rite that should be applied to the burial of bodies; it often prevents souls from returning to their bodies and drives them far away to the places of judgment.

Because of this, the souls of the dead are not easily raised, unless they are the souls of those whom we know...

<sig>L</sig>
<meta>catchword: know</meta>

<summary>This page outlines the ritual requirements for necromancy, specifying that blood, physical remains, and specific offerings like honey and milk are needed to draw souls back. It also identifies ideal locations for these rites, such as churchyards, execution sites, and scenes of recent violent deaths.</summary>
<keywords>necromancy, shadows, ritual offerings, burial rites, churchyards, violent death, expiation, hauntings</keywords>

<page-num>68</page-num>
<header>Of Occult Philosophy,</header>

...known to be evil, or who have died a violent death, and whose bodies lack a proper and rightful burial.

Now, although we have discussed these types of places, it will not always be safe or convenient <note>original: "commodious"</note> to travel to them. Instead, we should take some principal part of the remaining body <term>relict</term> <note>original: "relict"; the physical remains or fragments of the deceased</note> to whatever location is chosen. Using that part, one should create a ritual incense <note>original: "perfume"</note> in the proper way and perform other appropriate <note>original: "competent"</note> *rites*.

It should also be understood that because souls are a form of spiritual light, artificial lights can be very effective for raising departed *souls*. This is especially true if these lights are crafted from appropriate materials, prepared according to strict rules, and marked with matching inscriptions of names and seals.

Moreover, the things mentioned so far are not always enough to raise *souls*. This is because of a supernatural <note>original: "extranatural"</note> element of understanding and reason that exists above us, known only to *Heaven*, the *Destinies*, and their power.

Therefore, we must attract these *souls* using properly administered supernatural and celestial powers—specifically those things that resonate with the harmony of the *soul*, whether imaginative, rational, or intellectual. These include *voices*, *songs*, *sounds*, and *enchantments*, as well as religious elements such as *prayers*, *conjurations*, *exorcisms*, and other holy *rites*, which can be very conveniently applied to this purpose.

# <term>Heptameron</term> <note>original: "Heptameron"; from the Greek *hepta* (seven) and *hemera* (day). This title refers to the book's focus on the magic associated with the seven days of the week and their corresponding planetary spirits.</note>:

## OF
## PETER de ABANO
### PHILOSOPHER.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'I' within a square frame featuring ornate floral and foliate patterns.</image-desc>

In the previous book, which is the fourth book of *Agrippa*, enough has been said regarding magical ceremonies and initiations.

However, because he seems to have written for those who are learned and well-experienced in this art—and because he does not specifically address the ceremonies but instead speaks of them in general terms—it was therefore decided to add the *Magical Elements* of *Peter de Abano* <note>Peter de Abano (c. 1250–1316) was a famous Italian physician, astrologer, and professor whose name became associated with this influential grimoire of planetary magic.</note> here. This is so that those who are until now...

<summary>This page serves as the title page and introduction to the "Heptameron" by Peter de Abano, positioned as a practical supplement to Agrippa's theoretical fourth book.</summary>
<keywords>Heptameron, Peter de Abano, Agrippa, Magical Elements, ritual ceremonies, planetary magic, initiation</keywords>

<header>Magical Elements,</header>

...ignorant and have not tasted of magical rituals <note>original: "Superstitions"; in this era, the word often referred to specific religious or occult practices rather than just irrational beliefs</note>, so they may have them ready and know how to practice them. For we see in this book a kind of introduction to magical practices <note>original: "vanity"</note>; through these practical exercises, readers may see the distinct functions of spirits and how they may be drawn into conversation and communication. They will learn what must be done every day and every hour, and how these things should be read as if they were described syllable by syllable.

In brief, this book contains the principles of magical operations <note>original: "conveyances"</note>. But because the greatest power is attributed to Circles—which are essentially fortresses to keep the practitioners <note>original: "operators"</note> safe from evil spirits—we will first deal with the construction of a Circle.

## Concerning the Circle and Its Construction

The form of Circles is not always the same; it changes according to the order of the spirits that are to be called, as well as their locations, times, days, and hours. When making a Circle, you must consider the time of year, the day, and the hour you are working; which spirits you want to call, to which star and region they belong, and what their duties are.

Therefore, let three circles be made with a width <note>original: "latitude"</note> of nine feet, spaced a hand's breadth apart from one another. 

1. In the middle circle, first write the name of the hour in which you are working. 
2. Second, write the name of the Angel of the hour. 
3. Third, write the <term>sigil</term> <note>A symbolic signature or seal representing a specific spirit or angel.</note> of the Angel of the hour. 
4. Fourth, write the name of the Angel who rules the day on which you work, along with the names of his ministers. 
5. Fifth, write the name of the current season <note>original: "present time"</note>. 
6. Sixth, write the name of the spirits ruling that period of time and their presiding leaders. 
7. Seventh, write the name of the head of the zodiac <term>sign</term> ruling that part of 

<meta>catchword: time</meta>

<summary>This page explains the purpose of the manual as a practical guide for those new to magic and begins the specific instructions for drawing a protective magic Circle, detailing the seven names and sigils that must be inscribed within it.</summary>
<keywords>magic circle, planetary magic, sigil, angels, ritual protection, Peter de Abano, spirits, zodiac signs</keywords>

Eighthly, write the name of the Earth, according to the specific season <note>original: "part of time"</note> in which you work. Ninthly, and to complete the middle circle, write the name of the Sun and of the Moon according to the same rule of time; for as the time changes, so the names are to be altered. In the outermost circle, in the four angles, let the names of the presiding <note>original: "presidential"</note> angels of the air be drawn for the day you wish to perform this work; namely, the name of the King and his three ministers. Outside the circle, in the four angles, let <term>pentagons</term> <note>original: "Pentagones"; five-pointed stars used in ritual magic as symbols of protection and power.</note> be made. In the inner circle, let four divine names be written with crosses placed between them in the middle of the circle; specifically, toward the East let **Alpha** <note>The first letter of the Greek alphabet, representing the beginning.</note> be written, and toward the West let **Omega** <note>The last letter of the Greek alphabet, representing the end.</note> be written; and let a cross divide the middle of the circle. When the circle is finished in this way, according to the rule written above, you shall proceed.

## Concerning the names of the hours, and the angels ruling them.

It is also important to know that the angels rule the hours in a sequential order, according to the course of the heavens and the planets to which they are subject. Thus, the spirit that governs the day also rules the first hour of the day; the second spirit from this sequence governs the second hour; the third governs the third hour, and so on sequentially. When the seven planets and hours have completed their rotation <note>original: "revolution"</note>, it returns again to the first spirit which rules the day. Therefore, we shall first speak of the names of the hours.

| 2. Janor.         | 2. Barol.           |
| 3. Nasnia.        | 3. Thami.           |
| 4. Salla.         | 4. Athar.           |

## The Blessing <note>original: "Benediction"</note> of Perfumes.

> The God of *Abraham*, God of *Isaac*, and God of *Jacob*, bless here the creatures of these kinds [the incense and spices], that they may fulfill the power and virtue <note>original: "vertue"; here meaning the inherent spiritual or medicinal power</note> of their scents <note>original: "odours"</note>; so that neither the enemy, nor any false imagination, may be able to enter into them: through our Lord *Jesus Christ*, and so forth <note>original: "&c."</note>.

Then let them be sprinkled with holy water.

---

## The Exorcism of fire upon which the perfumes are to be put.

The fire which is to be used for fumigations <term>fumigations</term> <note>The ritual act of burning incense or herbs to produce smoke, intended to purify a space or attract specific spirits.</note> is to be in a new vessel of clay <note>original: "earth"</note> or iron; and let it be exorcised in this manner:

> I exorcise you, O creature of fire, by him by whom all things are made, that you immediately <note>original: "forthwith"</note> cast away every phantom <note>original: "phantasme"</note> from yourself, so that it shall not be able to do any harm in anything.

Then say,

> Bless, O Lord, this creature of fire, and sanctify it, that it may be blessed to set forth the praise of your holy name, and that no harm may come to the exorcists <note>original: "Exorcifers"</note> or spectators: through our Lord *Jesus Christ*, and so forth <note>original: "&c."</note>.

## Of the Garment and Pentacle.

Let it be a priest's garment, if possible; but if it cannot be obtained, let it be made of linen and be clean. Then take this pentacle <term>pentacle</term> <note>A magical talisman or geometric symbol, often drawn on parchment or engraved on metal, used to provide protection or to constrain spirits during a ritual.</note> made in the day and hour of *Mercury*, while the Moon is increasing <note>the waxing moon</note>, written on parchment made of a kid’s skin <note>skin from a young goat</note>. But first, let the Mass of the Holy Spirit <note>original: "holy Ghost"</note> be said over it, and let it be sprinkled with the water of baptism.

# Magic of Peter de Abano. <note>original: "Maga Of Peter, de Abano." Peter de Abano (c. 1250–1316) was an Italian philosopher and astrologer to whom this grimoire, the *Heptameron*, is attributed.</note> Magical Elements.
## On the method of operation.

Let the Moon be increasing and at a midpoint <note>original: "equal"; likely meaning in a balanced or favorable astrological aspect</note>, if it can be done then, and let her not be combust <note>term: "combust"; in astrology, a planet is 'combust' when it is so close to the sun that its influence is said to be burned up or weakened.</note>.

<margin>Wash me O Lord, etc.</margin>
The operator ought to be clean and purified for the space of nine days before the beginning of the work, and should confess their sins and receive the holy Communion. Let him have ready the incense <note>original: "perfume"</note> appropriate to the day on which he wishes to perform the work. He should also have holy water from a priest, a new earthen vessel with fire, a garment <note>original: "Vesture"</note>, and a pentacle; let all these things be rightly and duly consecrated and prepared.

Let one of the servants carry the earthen vessel full of fire and the incense, and let another carry the book, another the garment and pentacle, and let the master carry the sword. Over the sword, one Mass of the Holy Spirit <note>original: "Holy Ghost"</note> must be said; and on the middle of the sword, let this name Agla <note>original: "Alga"; this is almost certainly a printer's error for 'AGLA', a common kabbalistic acronym for the Hebrew 'Atah Gibor Le-olam Adonai', meaning 'You, O Lord, are mighty forever'.</note> † be written, and on the other side of it, the name † On <note>term: "On"; a divine name often found in medieval magic, sometimes linked to the Greek word for 'being' or the biblical city of Heliopolis.</note> †.

As he goes to the consecrated place, let him continually read litanies while the servants provide the responses. When he comes to the place where he will set up the circle, let him draw the lines of the circle as we have previously taught. After he has made it, let him sprinkle the circle with holy water, saying:
> You shall sprinkle me, O Lord, etc. <note>original: "Asperges me Domine, &c."; a reference to Psalm 51:7, traditionally used during the sprinkling of holy water in Christian liturgy.</note>

The master, therefore, must be purified through fasting, chastity, and abstinence from all luxury for the space of three whole days before the day of the operation. On the day he wishes to do the work, being clothed with pure garments and furnished with pentacles, incense, and other necessary things, let him enter the circle and call the angels from the four parts of the world who govern the seven planets, the seven days of the week, the colors, and the metals; you shall find their names in their proper places. With bended knees, invoking the said angels individually, let him say:
> O above-mentioned Angels, be helpers to my request, <note>original: "O Angeli supradicti, estote adjutores meae petitionis"</note>

<sig>M 2</sig>

<summary>This page details the rigorous physical and spiritual preparations required for the ritual, including a nine-day purification period, the specific tools needed (like a consecrated sword), and the initial steps for establishing a magical circle.</summary>
<keywords>Peter de Abano, ritual preparation, Agla, astrological timing, combust moon, purification, holy water, magic circle, angelic invocation, grimoire</keywords>

<page-num>78</page-num>
<header>Magical Elements,</header>

...and for my help in my affairs and requests. <note>original: "& in adjutorium mibi, in meis rebus & petitionibus." The word "mibi" is a common early modern printing error for the Latin "mihi," meaning "me."</note>

Then let him call the Angels from the four parts of the world who rule the air on the same day that he performs the work or experiment. Having specifically implored all the Names and Spirits written in the Circle, let him say: "O all you spirits, I adjure you and call you to witness by the throne of Adonay; by the Holy One, O God, the Strong, the Immortal, the Comforter, the Alpha and Omega; and by these three secret names—Agla, On, Tetragrammaton—that today you must fulfill what I desire." <note>original: "O vos omnes, adjuro atque contestor per sedem Adonay, per Hagios, ò Theos, Ischyros, Athanatos, Paracletos, Alpha & Omega, & per hæc tria nomina secreta, Agla, On, Tetragrammaton, quod hodie debeatis adimplere quod cupio."</note>

These things being performed, let him read the Conjuration assigned for the day on which he makes the experiment, as we have spoken of before. But if they are stubborn <note>original: "pertinacious"</note> and resistant <note>original: "refractory"</note> and will not submit themselves in obedience—neither to the Conjuration assigned to the day nor to the prayers made before—then use the Conjurations and Exorcisms that follow.

## An Exorcism *of the Spirits of the Air.*

"We, who were made in the image of God, endowed with the power of God, and created by His will, through the most powerful and strengthened name of God, *El*, the strong and wonderful, we exorcise you <note>original: "Nos facti ad imaginem Dei, dotati potentia Dei, & ejus facti voluntate, per potentissimum & corroboratum nomen Dei El, forte & admirabile, vos exorcizamus"</note> (here he shall name the Spirits he would have appear, of whatever order they may be) and we command you by Him who spoke and it was done, and by all the names of God, and by the name Adonay, El, Elohim, Elohe, Zebaoth, Elion, Escerchie, Jah, Tetragrammaton, Sadai, the Lord God Most High, we exorcise you and powerfully command you to appear immediately to us here beside the Circle in a beautiful form—specifically a human one—and without any deformity or twistedness. <note>original: "videlicet humana, & sine deformitate & tortuositate aliqua."</note>

Come, all you such spirits, because we command you by the names Y and V <note>These letters likely refer to parts of the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God.</note> which Adam heard and spoke; and by the name of God, *Agla*, which Lot <note>original: "Loth"</note> heard and was saved along with his family; and by the name *Joth*, which Jacob heard from the Angel struggling with him, and was delivered from the hand of his brother Esau; and by the name *Anephexeton*, which Aaron heard, and by speaking it was made wise; and by the name *Zebaoth*, which Moses named, and all the rivers and marshes of the land of Egypt <note>This refers to the first plague of Egypt, where Moses invoked God's name to turn the waters into blood.</note> [were turned into blood]..."

## An Exorcism of the Spirits of the Air.

We, being made in the image of God, endowed with power from God, and created according to His will, do exorcise you by the most mighty and powerful name of God, *El* <note>Hebrew: "God"</note>, strong and wonderful (*here he shall name the spirits he would have appear, of whatever order they may be*) and we command you by Him who spoke the word and it was done, and by all the names of God, and by the name *Adonai, El, Elohim, Elche Zebaoth, Elyon, Eserchie, Jath, Tetragrammaton, Shaddai*, Lord God most high: We exorcise you and powerfully command you that you immediately appear before us, here in front of this circle, in a fair human shape without any deformity or distortion <note>original: "tortuosity"</note>. # A Prayer to God, to be said in the four parts of the world, in the Circle.

*Amorule, Taneha, Latisten, Rabur, Teneba, Latisten, Escha, Aladia, Alpha & Omega, Leyste, Oriston, Adonai:* O my most merciful heavenly Father, have mercy upon me, although I am a sinner. Make the arm of your power appear in me this day (although I am your unworthy child) against these obstinate and pernicious spirits, so that I, by your will, may be made a contemplator of your divine works, and may be enlightened <note>original: "illustrated"</note> with all wisdom, and always worship and glorify your name. 

I humbly implore and beseech you that these spirits which I call by your judgment may be bound and constrained to come, and give true and perfect answers to those things which I shall ask them. Let them declare and show to us those things which shall be commanded of them by me or us, not hurting any creature, neither injuring nor terrifying me or my companions <note>original: "fellows"</note>, nor hurting any other creature, and frightening no man; but let them be obedient to my requests in all these things which I command them. 

Then let him stand in the middle of the Circle, and hold his hand towards the Pentacle <note>A magical talisman, often engraved with names of God and geometric symbols, used to constrain spirits</note>, and say: 

*By the Pentacle of Solomon I have called you, give me a true answer.* <note>original: "Per Pentaculum Salomonis advocavi, dent mihi responsum verum."</note>

Then let him say:

You of *Beralanensis, Baldachiensis, Paumachia*, and the seats of *Apologia* <note>These refer to the supposed celestial or infernal regions where the spirits reside</note>, by the magnanimous kings and powers, and the most mighty princes, the spirits <note>original: "genio"</note> of *Liachidae*, ministers of the infernal seats; *Primac*, the prince of the ninth cohort of the seat of *Apologia*: I invoke you, and in invoking you, I conjure you; and, being armed with the virtue of the supreme Majesty, and powerfully... <note>original: "Beralanensis, Baldachiensis, Paumachie & Apologiæ sedes, per Reges potestatesq; magnanimas, ac principes præpotentes, genio, Liachidæ, ministri tartareæ sedes: Primac, hic princeps sedis Apologiæ nona cohorte: Ego vos invoco, & invocando vos conjuro, atq; supernæ Majestatis munitus virtute, potente"</note>

<meta>catchword: powerfully</meta>

# *Visions and Apparitions.*

When these rituals are properly performed <note>original Latin: "Quibus rite peractis"</note>, endless visions will appear, including phantasms playing organs and all kinds of musical instruments. This is done by the spirits so that the companions, driven by fear, might leave the Circle, because the spirits can do nothing against the Master. After this, you will see an endless number of archers along with a vast multitude of horrible beasts, which position themselves as if they intended to devour the companions; yet let the companions fear nothing. Then let the Priest or Master, placing his hand on the Pentacle <term>a talisman or magical diagram, often inscribed on parchment or metal, used to constrain spirits</term>, say: "Let your wickedness flee from here, by the power of the banner of God." And then the spirits are compelled to obey the master, and the companions will see nothing more.

Then let the Exorcist say, stretching out his hand to the Pentacle: "Behold the Pentacle of Solomon, which I have brought before your presence. Behold the person of the exorcist in the middle of the Exorcism, who is perfectly protected by God, fearless and foresighted, who, being powerful in strength, has called and calls you by exorcising. Come therefore with haste by the power of these names: *Aye, Saraye; Aye, Saraye; Aye, Saraye*. Do not delay to come, by the eternal names of the living and true God: *Eloy, Archima, Rabur*; and by this present Pentacle, which powerfully commands you; and by the power of the celestial Spirits who are your lords; and by the person of the exorcist—being conjured, hasten to come and obey your master, who is called *Octinomos* <note>original Greek-derived name: "having eight names"</note>." 

When these things are done, whistle toward the four corners of the world. And you will immediately see great movements; and when you see them, say: "Why do you tarry? Why do you delay? What are you doing? Prepare yourselves and obey your master, in the name of the Lord *Bathat*, or *Vachat* rushing upon *Abrac*, coming upon him, *Abeor* upon *Aberer*."

Then they will immediately come in their own proper form. And when you see them near the Circle, show them the Pentacle covered with a sacred linen cloth <note>original: "syndone sacro"</note>; let it be uncovered, and let the master say: "Behold your conclusion <note>meaning the instrument of their subjection or the final word of the spell</note>; do not be disobedient." And suddenly you will see them in a peaceful form; and they will say to you: "Ask what you wish, for we are ready to fulfill all your commands, because the Lord has subjected us to these things."

## Visions and Apparitions.

When these things are properly performed, there will appear endless visions and illusions <term>original: phantasms</term>, the playing of organs and all kinds of musical instruments. This is done by the spirits so that, through terror, they might force the companions to leave the Circle, because they can do nothing against the Master. After this, you shall see an endless company of archers, along with a vast multitude of horrible beasts, which will position themselves as if they would devour the companions; nevertheless, fear nothing.

Then the Priest or Master, holding his hand toward the Pentacle <term>a protective talisman or magical symbol, often inscribed with holy names</term>, shall say: "Depart from here, these iniquities, by the power <note>original: "virtue"</note> of the banner of God." Then the spirits will be compelled to obey the Master, and the company shall see no more.

Then let the Exorcist, stretching out his hand to the Pentacle, say: "Behold the Pentacle of Solomon, which I have brought before your presence. Behold the person of the Exorcist in the middle of the exorcism, who is protected <note>original: "armed"</note> by God, fearless and well-provided, who powerfully invokes and calls you by exorcising. Come, therefore, with speed, in the

# Magical Elements

...by the power of these names, *Aye, Seraye, Aye, Seraye*; do not delay in coming, by the eternal names of the living and true God, *Eloy, Archima, Rabur*, and by the pentacle <term>a protective talisman or magical diagram</term> present here, which powerfully rules over you. I call you by the authority of the celestial spirits who are your lords, and by the person of the exorcist. Being conjured, hurry to come and submit in obedience to your master, who is called *Octinomos* <note>original Greek: "eight-named," a title sometimes associated with God or a powerful magician</note>. 

Once this is performed, there will be hissing sounds from the four corners of the world, and you will immediately see great movements. When you see them, say: "Why do you stay? Why do you delay? What are you doing? Prepare yourselves and be obedient to your master, in the name of the Lord *Bathat* or *Vachat* rushing upon *Abrac*, and *Abeor* coming upon *Aberer*."

Then they will immediately arrive in their proper form. When you see them before the circle, show them the pentacle covered with fine linen; uncover it and say: "Behold your conclusion <note>meaning: the symbol of your final submission or the end of your resistance</note>, if you refuse to be obedient." Suddenly, they will appear in a peaceful form and say, "Ask what you will, for we are prepared to fulfill all your commands, because the Lord has made us subject to this."

When the spirits have appeared, you shall say: "Welcome, spirits, or most noble kings, because I have called you through Him to whom every knee bows—in heaven, on earth, and under the earth—in whose hands are all the kingdoms of kings, and whom no one can contradict. Therefore, I bind you to remain friendly <note>original: "affable"</note> and visible before this circle, for as long and as steadily as I require. You shall not depart without my permission <note>original: "licence"</note> until you have truthfully and without any deceit performed my will, by the power of Him who set the boundaries of the sea, which it cannot pass, nor go beyond the law of His power—namely, of the most high God, Lord and King, who created all things. Amen."

† In the name of the Father, † and of the Son, † and of the Holy Ghost, go in peace to your places; let peace be between us and you, and be ready to come when you are called.

<margin>How you should then go to rest.</margin>

These are the things which *Peter de Abano* <note>also known as Pietro d'Abano (c. 1250–1316), an Italian philosopher and professor of medicine who was posthumously accused of magic</note> has spoken concerning *Magical Elements*.

# Of Peter de Abano.

But so that you may better understand the manner of constructing a circle, I will provide one diagram <note>original: "Scheme"</note>. If anyone wishes to make a circle in the springtime for the first hour of the Lord's Day <note>Sunday</note>, it must be done in the same manner as the following figure.

### The figure of a circle for the first hour of the Lord's Day, in springtime.

<image-desc>
A woodcut diagram of a magical circle used in ritual magic. The circle is composed of several concentric rings and a central cross.
- Around the exterior of the circle are four five-pointed stars and two labels: "Cynabal" to the left and "Incense" <note>original Latin: "Tus"</note> to the right.
- The outermost ring contains various names of spirits or angels arranged clockwise: "King Varcan" <note>original Latin: "Varcan Rex"</note> at the top, followed by "Michael" (accompanied by magical symbols), "Michael," "Dardiel," "Huratapel," "Talui," "Andas," "Caracasa," "Cor," "Amatic," "Commissoros," "Spugliguel," "Amaday," "Abraym," "Aguista," and "Yaym."
- The second ring contains the four sacred names "Tetragrammaton" <note>the four-letter Hebrew name of God</note>, "Adonai" <note>Lord</note>, "Eloi" <note>God</note>, and "Agla" <note>a Hebrew acronym for "You are strong forever, O Lord"</note>, each separated by a small cross.
- The innermost circle is divided into four quadrants by a cross. The top two quadrants contain the word "Alpha." The bottom two quadrants contain the word "and" <note>original Latin: "et"</note> and a stylized Greek letter Omega, representing "The Beginning and the End."
</image-desc>

It now remains for us to explain the week and its various days; we begin with the Lord's Day.

## The Conjuration of the Lord's Day <note>Sunday</note>

> I conjure and confirm upon you, you strong and holy Angels of God, in the name of Adonai, Eheieh, Eheieh, Eheieh <note>original: *Adonay, Eye, Eye, Eya*</note>, who is he who was, and is, and is to come, Eheieh Abray <note>names of God</note>; and in the name of Shaddai, Kadosh, Kadosh, Kadosh <note>original: *Saday, Cados, Cados, Cados*; meaning Almighty, Holy, Holy, Holy</note>, sitting on high upon the Cherubim; and by the great Name of God himself, strong and powerful, who is exalted above all Heavens, *Eheieh Asher Eheieh* <note>original: *Eye Saray*; a name of God often translated as "I am that I am"</note>, maker of the World, who created the World, the Heaven, the Earth, the Sea, and all that is in them on the first day, and sealed them with his holy Name Phaa; and by the name of the holy Angels, who rule in the fourth heaven <note>original: *Heaven*</note>, and serve before the most mighty Salamia, an Angel great and honorable; and by the name of his Star, which is the Sun <note>original: *Sol*</note>; and by his Sign; and by the immense name of the living God, and by all the names mentioned before, I conjure you, Michael, oh great Angel, who are chief Ruler of the Lord's day; and by the name Adonai, the God of Israel, who has created the world and all that is within it, that you labor for me, and fulfill all my petitions, according to my will and desire, in my cause and business.

The Spirits of the Air of the Lord's day are under the North wind; their nature is to procure gold, gems, carbuncles <note>a red gemstone, such as a garnet or ruby</note>, and riches; to cause one to obtain favor and benevolence; to resolve the hostilities of men; to raise men to honors; and to bring or remove illnesses <note>original: "carry or take away infirmities"</note>. But in what manner they appear has been described already in the former book of Magical Ceremonies. <gloss>See pages 41 to 45 of the 4th Book.</gloss>

## The Conjuration of Monday.

### Of *Peter de Abano* <note>original: Petrus de Abano (1250–1316), an Italian philosopher and astrologer accused of magic.</note>

## The Conjuration of Monday

I conjure and confirm upon you, you strong and good Angels, in the name *Adonai, Adonai, Adonai, Eye, Eye, Eye, Cados, Cados, Cados, Achim, Achim, Ja, Ja, strong Ja* <note>These are transliterated Hebrew divine names: Adonai (Lord), Ehyeh (I am), Kadosh (Holy), Achim (Brothers/Powers), and Yah (God).</note>, who appeared on Mount Sinai with the glorification of King *Adonai, Shaddai, Zebaoth, Anathay, Ya, Ya, Ya, Maranatha, Abim, Jeia* <note>Shaddai (Almighty), Zebaoth (of Hosts), Maranatha (Our Lord comes).</note>, who created the sea and all lakes and waters on the second day—those which are above the heavens and in the earth—and sealed the sea in his high name and gave it bounds beyond which it cannot pass. 

And by the names of the Angels who rule in the first Legion, who serve *Orphaniel*, a great, precious, and honorable Angel; and by the name of his Star, which is the Moon <note>The original text uses the symbol for Saturn (♄), but the context of Monday and Gabriel indicates this is likely a printing error for the Moon (☽).</note>; and by all the names aforesaid, I conjure you, *Gabriel*, who are the chief Ruler of Monday, the second day, that for me you labor and fulfill, etc. *Ask what you would have.*

The Spirits of the Air of Monday are subject to the West Wind, which is the wind of the Moon. Their nature is to give silver; to convey things from place to place; to make horses swift; and to disclose the secrets of persons, both present and future. But in what manner they appear, you may see in the former book.

### The Conjuration of Tuesday

<margin>I Conjure.</margin>
I conjure and confirm my command upon you, you strong and holy angels, by the names *Ya, Ya, Ya, He, He, He, Va, Hy, Hy, Ha, Ha, Va, Va, Va, An, An, An, Aie, Aie, Aie, El, Ay, Elibra, Eloim, Eloim* <note>These are phonetic transliterations of Hebrew divine names used for their perceived vibrational power</note>; and by the name of that high God who made the dry land appear and called it Earth, and brought forth herbs and trees out of the same, and sealed the same with his precious, honorable, fearful, and holy name; and by the name of the angels ruling in the fifth heaven, who serve *Acimoy*, a great angel, strong, powerful, and honorable; and by the name of his star, which is *Mars*; and by the names aforesaid, I conjure upon you, *Samael*, who are a great angel and the chief ruler of Tuesday; and by the name *Adonay*, the living and true God, that you labor for me and fulfill my requests. <note>The "&c." indicates the practitioner should insert their specific petition here.</note>

The Spirits of the Air of Tuesday are under the East Wind. Their nature is to cause wars, mortality, death, and combustions <note>original: "combustions"; likely referring to fires or violent outbursts</note>; and to provide two thousand soldiers at a time; and to bring death, infirmities, or health. The manner of their appearing you may see in the former book.

### The Conjuration of Wednesday.

I conjure and confirm upon you, you strong, holy, and potent angels, in the name of the most strong, most dreadful, and blessed *Ja, Adonay, Eloim, Saday, Saday, Saday, Eie, Eie, Eie, Afamie, Afaraie* <note>original: Latin and Hebrew names of God used as words of power</note>; and in the name of *Adonay*, the God of Israel, who created the two great lights to distinguish the day from the night; and by the name of all the angels serving in the second host, before *Tetra*, a great, strong, and powerful angel; and by the name of his star, which is Mercury; and by the name of the seal which is sealed by God, most mighty and honorable. ### Of Peter de Abano <note>original: "Petrus de Abano," a 13th-century Italian philosopher and astrologer reputed to be a magician.</note>

The Spirits of the Air of Wednesday are subject to the Southwest wind. Their nature is to provide all metals and to reveal all earthly things—past, present, and to come. They are able to pacify judges, to give victories in war, and to rebuild <note>original: "re-edifie"</note> and teach experiments and all lost <note>original: "decayed"</note> sciences. They can change bodies composed of the elements from one state into another, provided certain conditions are met. They have the power to give sickness or health, to raise the poor, and to cast down the powerful. They can bind or release spirits and open locks or bolts. These kinds of spirits can perform the tasks of other spirits, though not with the same absolute power, but rather through specialized virtue or knowledge. The manner in which they appear has been described previously.

# Magical Elements

But because there are no Angels of the Air to be found above the fifth heaven, therefore on Thursday say the prayers following in the four parts of the world <note>The four cardinal directions: East, West, North, and South.</note>.

->At the East.<-
O great and most high God, honored through infinite ages.
<note>original: "O Deus magne & excelse, & honorate per infinita secula."</note>

->At the West.<-
O wise, bright, and just God, of divine clemency: I beseech you, most holy Father, that I may today fulfill and perfectly understand my petition, my work, and my labor. You who live and reign through infinite ages of ages, Amen.

->At the North.<-

->The Perfume of Thursday.<-
Saffron.

## The Conjuration of Friday

I conjure and confirm upon you, strong, holy, and powerful Angels, in the name of *On, Hey, Heya, Ja, Je, Adonay, Saday* <note>These are various mystical names for God derived from Hebrew</note>; and in the name of *Saday*, who created the four-footed beasts and creeping animals, and humans on the sixth day, and gave to Adam power over all animals; wherefore blessed be the name of the Creator in his place. 

And by the names of the Angels serving in the third army, before *Dagiel*, a great Angel and a strong and powerful prince; and by the name of the Star which is Venus; and by her seal, which is indeed holy; and by the names aforesaid, I conjure upon you, *Anael*, who are the chief ruler of the sixth day, that you labor for me, etc. <note>original Latin: "Conjuro & confirmo super vos Angeli fortes... ut pro me labores."</note> As written before in the Conjuration of Sunday.

## The Conjuration of Friday

I conjure and confirm my authority over you, you strong, holy, and powerful Angels; in the name *On, Hey, Heya, Yah, Ye, Adonai, Shaddai* <note>original: "On, Hey, Heya, Ja, Je, Adonay, Saday" — these are various Hebrew names and mystical titles for God</note>, and in the name *Shaddai*, who created four-footed beasts, and creeping things, and man on the sixth day, and gave to *Adam* power over all creatures. Therefore, blessed be the name of the Creator in his dwelling place. I conjure you by the name of the Angels serving in the third host, before *Dagiel*, a great Angel and a strong and powerful prince; and by the name of the Star which is *Venus*, and by his Seal which is holy, and by all the names mentioned before, I conjure you, *Anael*, who are the chief ruler of the sixth day, that you labor for me, etc.

The Spirits of the Air of Friday are subject to the West Wind. Their nature is to give silver; to excite men and incline them to luxury <note>In this context, "luxury" refers to lechery or intense physical desire</note>; to reconcile enemies through passion; to arrange marriages; to allure men to love women; to cause or take away infirmities; and to do all things which involve motion.

### The Conjuration of Saturday.

I conjure and confirm my command upon you, Capbriel (or Cassiel), Machator, and Seraquiel, strong and powerful Angels; and by the name Adonay, Adonay, Adonay, Eie, Eie, Eie, Acim, Acim, Acim, Cados, Cados, Ina, or Ima, Ima, Sallay, Ja, Sar <note>These are Hebrew-derived names of God and power often used in ritual magic</note>, Lord and maker of the world, who rested on the seventh day; and by him who of his good pleasure gave the same to be observed by the Children of Israel throughout their generations, that they should thoroughly keep and sanctify the same, to have thereby a good reward in the world to come; and by the names of the Angels serving in the seventh host, before Booel, a great Angel and powerful Prince; and by the name of his Star, which is Saturn; and by his holy Seal; and by the names spoken before, I conjure you, Capbriel, who are the chief ruler of the seventh day, which is the Sabbath day, that you work for me, etc.

The Spirits of the Air of Saturday are subject to the Southwest wind. Their nature is to sow discord, hatred, and evil thoughts; to give permission freely to slay and kill everyone, and to lame or maim every limb. Their manner of appearing is declared in the former book.

But this should be observed in passing: the first hour of the day, in every country and in every season whatsoever, is to be assigned to the sunrise, when the sun first appears rising on the horizon. <page-num>111</page-num>
<header>Of the Nature of Spirits.</header>

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut headpiece featuring two grotesque faces, foliage, and a central vase-like element.</image-desc>

### ISAGOGE <note>original: "Isagoge"; a Greek term meaning an introductory manual or primer.</note>
An Introductory Discourse on the nature of such Spirits as are active within the sublunary <note>The region below the moon, traditionally considered the realm of change and decay in ancient cosmology.</note> bounds; their origin, names, offices, illusions, power, prophecies, and miracles; and how they may be expelled and driven away.

By Georgius Pictorius of Villingen, Doctor of Medicine. <note>original: "Geo. Pictorius Villingannus Dr. in Physick." Pictorius (c. 1500–1569) was a German physician and writer on magic.</note>

In a Discourse between
## CASTOR and POLLUX.

**Castor.** <image-desc>Large decorative initial capital 'T' with intricate floral and foliate designs.</image-desc> The Greeks report that Castor and Pollux both proceeded from one egg; but I scarcely believe this, because of the difference in your minds. For you aspire to the heavens, but she <note>Likely referring to a personified quality or perhaps a misprint for "he" in the original, referring to the earthly nature.</note> meditates upon the earth and slaughters.

**Pollux.** And perhaps that is where the argument originated: that the liberty of lying was always assigned to the Greeks.

**Castor.** Principally.

<summary>This page serves as the title and introduction to a dialogue by Georgius Pictorius concerning the nature, powers, and exorcism of spirits inhabiting the earthly realm. It opens with a mythological exchange between the figures Castor and Pollux regarding Greek reliability and their own origins.</summary>

<page-num>112</page-num>
<header>A Discourse</header>

*Pollux.* But it is not to be supposed that the Greeks are boastful in all things; but rather like many others when they speak from a tripod <note>original: "three-footed thing"; a reference to the Delphic Oracle's seat, implying prophetic or inspired speech</note>; of which also the poet *Ovid* speaks in verse:

---
->The Greeks do not invent everything. <note>original: "Nec fingunt omnia Græci."</note><-
---

<margin>Man is a God to man.</margin> *Castor.* In this proverb I protest they are most truthful, without any exception: that is, *one man to another is a spirit* <note>original Greek: "ἄνθρωπο ἀνθρώπω δαιμόνιον"</note>, or rather, *one man to another is a devil.*

*Pollux.* Why do you believe this to be most true, *Castor*?

<margin>Man is a devil to man.</margin> *Castor.* Truly, that man to man is a devil and a ravening wolf, daily events do most certainly prove. We need only note the treacheries that one man invents daily against another: the robberies, thefts, plunderings, rapes, slaughters, deceits, adulteries, and a hundred vipers of this nature. Fathers persecute their sons with a serpentine and poisonous biting; one friend seeks to devour another, and even the guest is not safe with his host.

*Pollux.* I confess what you speak is the truth; but from what I hear, you misunderstand the etymology <note>original: "Etymologie"; the study of the origin of words</note> of the word used in this proverb. For <term>Demon</term> <note>original: "Demon" or "Daemon"; in Greek philosophy, this referred to a tutelary spirit or divine power, not necessarily an evil entity</note> here is not a horrible or hateful name, but the name of one who administers 

<margin>Pliny, Book 2, Chapter 7.</margin> help or succor to another, and whom *Pliny* calls a god.

*Castor.* Therefore, do you affirm that the word <term>Demon</term> in this proverb signifies anything other than a cunning and malicious accuser?

*Pollux.* You have not missed the mark. For that there are more <term>Demons</term> than that earthly <note>original: "sublunary"; literally "under the moon," meaning belonging to the physical world</note> one which you understand, everyone may easily perceive who has not carelessly read the opinions of the most excellent *Plato*.

*Castor.* I desire, therefore, that you would not conceal his writings, but let me grasp the core <note>original: "marrow"</note> of them.

*Pollux.* I will embrace your desire, for truly I delight to discuss this subject with you. Take note, therefore, and give me your attention.

<meta>The text concludes with the catchword "Plato" for the following page.</meta>

<summary>Castor and Pollux debate the proverb "Man is a demon to man," contrasting the cynical view of human cruelty with the Platonic definition of a "demon" as a helpful or divine spirit. Pollux prepares to explain Plato's specific teachings on the hierarchy of spirits.</summary>
<keywords>Pollux, Castor, Plato, Pliny, Ovid, Demon, Etymology, Human Nature, Greek Philosophy, Ethics</keywords>

--- Page 133 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature and hierarchy of spirits, following the previous classification of celestial and elemental entities.</meta>

<margin>Spirits.</margin>
<margin>The first degree of Spirits.</margin>
Plato divided the orders of spirits <note>original: "Devils or Spirits"</note> into three degrees. Just as they are distinct in the greatness of their dignity, they are also different in the distance and location of their places. The first order he ascribes to those spirits whose bodies are nourished by the most pure element of air, woven and joined together, in a manner, as if with splendid threads. They do not have enough of the element of fire to be clearly visible to the sight, nor do they participate enough in the element of earth to be touched or felt. They inhabit the celestial theater, attending and waiting on their Prince—who cannot be described by any human tongue—and they never act beyond the commands of the most wise God.

<margin>The second degree.</margin>
The second degree is derived from those spirits which Apuleius <note>A 2nd-century Roman philosopher and author of "The Golden Ass."</note> calls "rational animals," who are passive in their minds and eternal in their time. This refers to the apostate <note>rebellious or fallen</note> spirits spread abroad from the borders of the Moon down to us, under the dominion of their Prince, Beelzebub. Before the fall of Lucifer, these spirits had pure, clarified bodies; now, following their transgression, they wander up and down like the former group, but in the form of an airy quality.

### *Castor.*
I do not believe these are the ones referred to in the Greek Proverb, for these spirits cause harm and are the accusers and betrayers of men. But please, proceed.

<margin>The third degree.</margin>
<margin>Plato called a daemon.</margin>
<margin>Aristotle.</margin>
### *Pollux.*
The third degree of spirits is of a divine nature, which Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure associated with wisdom and alchemy.</note> calls "a divine miracle to man," provided that man does not degenerate from the kingly state of his original form. Because of this, the Greeks and Plato have called this kind *Daemons* <note>original: "Dæmons," meaning a guiding spirit or lesser deity, not necessarily an evil "demon" in the modern sense.</note>, which is to say, "God-like." This is so that man may be like God and be helpful and beneficial to one another. Thus (according to the Syrian witness), we know that Plato himself was called a *Daemon* because he had published many things concerning very high matters for the good of the Commonwealth. Likewise, Aristotle was called the same because he disputed so extensively regarding sublunary <note>things below the moon; earthly matters.</note> affairs and all things subject to motion and the senses. Homer calls both God and evil spirits *Daemons* without making a distinction between them.

<summary>This page outlines Plato's three-fold hierarchy of spirits, ranging from pure celestial attendants to fallen airy spirits under Beelzebub, and finally to "daemons" or god-like human intellects like Plato and Aristotle.</summary>
<keywords>Plato, hierarchy of spirits, Beelzebub, Lucifer, Daemons, Aristotle, Apuleius, pneumatology, Greek philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 134 ---
<meta>This page continues a dialogue between Castor and Pollux regarding the nature and hierarchy of spirits, specifically focusing on those Plato termed "Lunaries."</meta>

**Castor.** You have committed the ship to the waves, Pollux; therefore, do not cease your progress. Declare something more concerning the office and employment of these spirits, to whom Plato attributes the second degree and calls them **Lunaries** <note>Spirits associated with the Moon or the sublunary sphere, the region between Earth and the Moon</note>.

**Pollux.** What shall I say?

**Castor.** In the first place, explain why you previously called these spirits "cunning and all-knowing accusers."

<margin>Why the devil is said to have much knowledge.</margin>
**Pollux.** Saint Augustine explains this difficulty. He says that a **Devil** <note>original: *Diabolus*, often interpreted in Greek as "accuser" or "slanderer"</note> possesses such a cunning and knowledgeable quickness and vivacity in his deceitful wit that, through the fitting and agreeable mixing of the "seeds" of the elements, he knows the secrets and unknown virtues of men. He understands how things that are naturally produced slowly and leisurely over time can be brought to pass much sooner by his own art, either by speeding up or forcing the works of nature. He gives an example of this in the wise men of Pharaoh, who immediately produced frogs and serpents at the King's command—creatures which nature procreates much more slowly.

**Castor.** You have answered the question excellently, Pollux. But add something concerning the origin of those spirits who resist and refuse virtues; for I have often doubted, being persuaded that such **Erinnyes** <note>original: *Erynnnes*; Furies or avenging spirits from Greek mythology</note> as come from God do not emerge from the earth.

<margin>Book 2, Distinction 7.</margin>
**Pollux.** The Ecclesiastical Scripture mentions their origin everywhere, but I will resolve such a doubt. There are many and various opinions among writers, but most commonly Peter Lombard, in his *Book of Sentences*, draws his arguments from Saint Augustine’s commentary on Genesis. Specifically, he argues that before his fall, the Devil was an Archangel and had a fine, delicate body composed by God out of the serenity and purest matter of the sky and air. However, after his fall from being an Archangel, he was made an **Apostate** <note>One who has abandoned their religious faith or principles</note>, and his body was no longer fine and subtle. Instead, his body was altered so that it might suffer the effects of a more gross substance, taking on the quality of the more obscure, dark, and [heavy] spirits...

<summary>This page features a dialogue explaining the superior natural knowledge of demons—who use their understanding of "elemental seeds" to perform seemingly miraculous feats—and discusses the theological origin of the Devil's physical form following his fall from grace.</summary>
<keywords>demonology, Saint Augustine, Peter Lombard, Lunary spirits, Pharaoh's magicians, fall of Lucifer, elemental seeds, Erinnyes</keywords>

### Of the Nature of Spirits.

Spirits of the Air—*which body <note>referring to the collective group of fallen angels</note> was also stricken and astonished with the raging madness of pride—did draw away very many who were then Angels with him into his service and bondage, so that they might be made Devils. For him, they exercise their servile duties in this troublesome world, and they compel the inhabitants therein, or rather entice them. To this purpose, they undertake various endeavors and attempt various and manifold horrible studies that are abominable unto God; they serve in slavery and thraldom to Beelzebub their Prince, and are held in most strong captivity.*

**Castor:** What? Do we have the account of the fall of this Archangel nowhere else in Holy Writ <note>the Bible</note>, but in the writings of St. Augustine?

**Pollux:** We also have the account of the fall of other Angels.

**Castor:** Where?

<margin>The fall of Lucifer in Scripture.</margin>
**Pollux:** In Isaiah <note>original: "Esaias"</note>—to whom you should give the honor of an Evangelist rather than a Prophet, because he so fully and plainly foretold of Christ and his kingdom—he makes mention of this in his 14th Chapter. And we also have them spoken of by the Apostle Peter, when he says, *God spared not his Angels which sinned* (2 Peter 2:4).

**Castor:** Have the Devils a specific place appointed to them by God which they inhabit?

<margin>What place the devils have appointed.</margin>
**Pollux:** Peter, the head of the Church, in the place before quoted, affirms them to be cast headlong into hell, reserved in the chains of darkness <note>original: "chains of hell"</note>, from whence (as Cortesius <note>Paolo Cortesi, a Renaissance theologian</note> says) they never go out, *unless it be to tempt, provoke, and delude men.* But St. Augustine, the Champion of Christ, in his book *The Agony of a Christian*, teaches *that these kinds of Spirits do inhabit the sublunary region* <note>the area of space below the moon; the Earth's atmosphere</note>. *And in his 49th Epistle, he sets forth that the most dark and obscure part of the Air is predestined unto them as a prison, so that they may more closely cast their nets of enticing and detaining.*

**Castor:** Origen has taught *that the punishments of the Devils are appointed only for a time <note>referring to the doctrine of universal reconciliation, or Apocatastasis</note>; what do you say to this?*

<margin>The torments of the devils are everlasting.</margin>
**Pollux:** What shall I say? Unless I should bewail and deplore the opinion of so great a man.

**Castor:** Why should you do so?
