If the Lord of the first house is with the Lord of the second house, the item shall be found. But if the Lord of the second house is in the eighth house, it shall not be found. If the Lord of the first house is in the second house, it may be found after much labor. But if you wish to know what kind of object has been stolen, look to the Lord of the second house. If that planet is *Saturn*, the item is made of lead or iron, or it is a kettle, a trivet <note>original: "triver"; a three-legged stand for supporting a pot over a fire.</note>, a garment, some black object, or leather. If it is *Jupiter*, then it is something white, such as tin or silver, or something with white and yellow veins. The *Sun* signifies gold and precious pearls. *Mars* signifies things belonging to the fire. *Venus* signifies women’s belongings, such as gloves, rings, and beautiful ornaments. The *Moon* signifies beasts, such as horses, mules, etc., as well as perfumes and waxes <note>original: "wars"; likely a transcription error for "wax" or "wares."</note>. *Mercury* signifies money, books, writings, pictures, or garments of many colors.

If you wish to know how many thieves there were, look to the Lord of the sixth house. If he is in the second house, or positioned with the Lord of the second house, there were many thieves. If they are in the third house, the siblings or kinsmen of the <term>Querent</term> <note>The person asking the question of the astrologer.</note> have committed the theft.

If you wish to know whether the thief still remains in the town: if the planets are in <term>succedent houses</term> <note>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses, which follow the "angles" and suggest a middle distance.</note>, he has not gone far away. But if they are in <term>cadent houses</term> <note>The 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses, which "fall away" from the angles and suggest movement or distance.</note>, he is far remote.

If you desire to know toward what country the thief has fled, see what zodiac sign the Lord of the seventh house is in. 
* If he is in *Aries*, he is in the middle of the East. 
* If in *Taurus*, he is in the South toward the East. 
* If in *Gemini*, he is in the West toward the South. 
* If in *Cancer*, he is due North. 
* If in *Leo*, he is in the East toward the North. 
* If in *Virgo*, he is in the South toward the West. 
* If in *Libra*, he is due West. 
* If in *Scorpio*, he is in the North near the West. 
* If in *Sagittarius*, he is in the East near the North. 
* If in *Capricorn*, he is due South. 
* If in *Aquarius*, he is in the West toward the North.

## Questions of the Ninth House.

Concerning long journeys, see if the Lord of the eighth house has good planets with him. And if the Lord of the ninth has a good planet near him, he shall escape; but if evil, he shall be captured. 

If *Venus* is in the ninth, or with the Lord of the ninth, the way will be good, because he shall have comfort from women. And if *Mercury* is in the ninth, and the Lord of the ninth is with good planets, the way will be very good; and if he is with evil planets, it will be evil. And the same is said of the Moon as of Mercury. If the *Dragon's Tail* is in the ninth, he will meet with thieves or some evil people. And if the *Dragon's Head* <note>term: Dragon's Head; the North Node of the Moon, traditionally associated with gain or positive outcomes.</note> is in the ninth, the way will be good, because he shall be accompanied by noblemen. And in this manner may you judge in the third house concerning short journeys.

To determine when a journey will be accomplished, look to the Lord of the ninth house <note>The "Lord" is the planet that rules the zodiac sign appearing on the cusp of that house</note>. Judge according to his strength or weakness, because the place where he is positioned signifies years, months, or days. You shall judge the length of his stay and approximately what time he will return by converting the years of the Lord of the ninth into days; he shall remain for as many days as the Planet signifies in its current position. Alternatively: weak Angles <note>Angles are the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses; "weak" refers to planets placed there in signs where they lack power</note> signify a speedy journey, middle Angles signify a journey of average length; the Lord of the ninth is judged similarly according to his position.

And this is what I have to say concerning his return.

If you want to know whether he shall return from his journey with an unfinished voyage or not, see if the Moon is joined with the Lord of the first, the third, or the ninth house. If the Planet of that house is in its "fall" <note>A position in the zodiac where a planet's influence is at its weakest</note>, he shall return with an incomplete voyage. If the Moon is in her "exaltation" <note>A position where a planet's influence is most powerful</note>, the journey shall be completed quickly. If there are two strong Planets and one "Cadent" <note>original: "Cadent"; refers to planets in the 3rd, 6th, 9th, or 12th houses, which are considered weaker</note>, the journey shall be made; but if one is strong and another is in its fall, he shall turn back.

### Questions of the Tenth House.

If you would know whether you shall receive any honor or benefit from a King, Bishop, or Lord, look to the first House and the ninth House, and their Lords. If the Lord of the first is in the ninth, or with the Lord of the ninth, or with any other beneficial Planet; or if the Lord of the ninth is in the first, or with the Lord of the first, or with any other beneficial Planet—such as *Venus*, *Jupiter*, or the *Dragon's Head* <note>The North Node of the Moon, considered highly favorable</note>; or if any of these are the Lord of the ninth or the Lord of the first, the person shall receive honor and benefits from those superiors.

And if you would know whether he shall receive this honor in his own country or in a foreign country, see if the Lord of the ninth is in the "Angles" <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>; if so, it shall be in his own country. If the Lord is in "succedent" houses <note>The 2nd, 5th, 8th, or 11th houses, which follow the Angles</note>, it shall be nearby; but if in "cadent" houses, it shall be very far away.

### *Questions of the Eleventh House.*

If you would know when it is good to set forth a ship to sea, look to the Ascendant <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question.</note>. You may judge a horse in this same manner, if a question is asked about one.

If any misfortune is predicted for the ship, or if she is likely to be drawn into danger, then set her out when a fixed sign is ascending, or when the Moon is in the third, fifth, eighth, ninth, or tenth house or mansion.

<meta>This is the title page and introductory front matter for the "Arbatel of Magic," establishing the spiritual and ethical framework for the work that follows.</meta>

# Arbatel <note>original: ארבעתאל; a Hebrew-derived name likely meaning "The Fourfold God" or "The Altar of God"</note>
## Of the MAGIC of the Ancients.
### The Greatest Study of Wisdom.

> *In all things, ask counsel of the Lord; and do not think, speak, or do anything in which God is not your counselor.*

Proverbs 11 <note>specifically verse 13</note>
> *He who walks deceitfully reveals secrets: but he who is of a faithful spirit conceals the matter.*

# ARBATEL of MAGIC:
## OR,
### The Spiritual Wisdom of the Ancients,
#### Both the Wise Men of the People of God <note>referring to Biblical figures and prophets</note>,
#### and the Magi of the Gentiles <note>referring to the wise men of non-Jewish nations, such as the Persians or Greeks</note>: for the illustration of the glory of God and His love to Mankind.

Now first of all produced out of darkness into the light, against all evil magicians <note>original: "caco-Magicians," from the Greek 'kakos' meaning bad or evil</note> and those who despise the gifts of God; for the benefit and delight of all those who truly and piously love the creatures of God, and use them with thanksgiving, to the honor of God and the profit of themselves and their neighbors.

<summary>This title page introduces the Arbatel as a work of "spiritual wisdom" intended to distinguish true, godly magic from evil practices, emphasizing the importance of divine counsel and secrecy.</summary>
<keywords>Arbatel, Magic, Wisdom, Magi, Gentiles, Caco-magicians, Piety, Proverbs 11:13</keywords>

--- Page 188 ---
<meta>This page introduces the structure of the "Arbatel of Magic," outlining the nine specific volumes or "Tomes" that comprise the work.</meta>

<page-num>68</page-num>
<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

## Arbatel of Magic.

Containing nine volumes and seven sets of seven <note>original: "Septenaries"</note> Aphorisms.

The first is called *Isagoge* <note>Greek: "Introductory Guide"</note>, or, A Book of the Institutions of Magic: or, *of spiritual matters* <note>original: "τῆς πνευματικῆς"</note>, which in forty-nine aphorisms contains the most general instructions of the whole Art.

The second is Microcosmic Magic: what the Microcosm <note>The "little world," referring to man as a reflection of the universe.</note> has achieved magically by his spirit and the guiding genius assigned to him from his birth—that is, spiritual wisdom—and how the same is performed.

The third is Olympic Magic: in what manner a person may act and be acted upon by the spirits of Olympus.

The fourth is Hesiodic <note>original: "Heliodiacal," likely referring to the poet Hesiod</note> and Homeric Magic, which teaches operations performed by spirits called *Cacodæmones* <note>Greek: "Evil spirits" or "harsh spirits," though here suggested to be less than fully hostile.</note>, as if they were not entirely enemies to mankind.

The fifth is Roman or Sibylline Magic, which acts and operates with Tutelary <note>Protective or guardian spirits.</note> Spirits and Lords, to whom the entire globe of the earth is distributed. This is *greatly distinguished Magic* <note>original: "valde insignis Magia"</note>. To this also the doctrine of the Druids is referred.

The sixth is Pythagorean Magic, which only acts with spirits who are given the teaching of the Arts, such as Physics <note>original: "Physick," meaning natural science or medicine.</note>, Medicine, Mathematics, Alchemy, and similar kinds of arts.

The seventh is the Magic of *Apollonius* <note>Apollonius of Tyana, a famous first-century philosopher and reputed magician.</note> and the like, and agrees with Roman and Microcosmic Magic; it has only this unique quality: it has power over the spirits that are hostile to mankind.

The eighth is Hermetic—that is, *Egyptian Magic*—and does not differ much from Divine Magic.

The ninth is that wisdom which depends solely upon the Word of God; and this is called *Prophetic Magic*.

<summary>This page outlines the nine "Tomes" or divisions of the Arbatel, categorizing magic into various branches including Olympic, Pythagorean, Hermetic, and Prophetic systems.</summary>
<keywords>Arbatel, Isagoge, Microcosm, Olympic Magic, Cacodæmones, Sibylline Magic, Druids, Pythagorean Magic, Apollonius of Tyana, Hermetic Magic, Prophetic Magic</keywords>

### The First Volume of the Book of
## Arbatel of Magic,
->TITLED<-
## ISAGOGE. <note>original: "ISAGOGE" — a Greek term meaning "Introduction" or "Elementary Treatise."</note>

<image-desc>Large decorated initial 'I' with floral and geometric patterns.</image-desc>
In the Name of the Creator of all things, both visible and invisible, who reveals his mysteries out of his treasures to those who call upon him, and who fatherly and mercifully bestows those secrets upon us without measure. May he grant unto us, through his only begotten Son Jesus Christ our Lord, his ministering spirits—the revealers of his secrets—so that we may write this Book of Arbatel. This book concerns the greatest secrets which are lawful for man to know, and how to use them without offending God. Amen.

### The First Septenary <note>original: "Septenary" — a group or set of seven.</note> of Aphorisms:
The First Aphorism.

Whoever desires to know secrets, let him know how to keep secret things secretly. He must reveal those things that are meant to be revealed and seal those things which are meant to be sealed. Do not give holy things to dogs, nor cast your pearls before swine. <note>A reference to Matthew 7:6, advising the reader to protect sacred knowledge from those who would disrespect or misuse it.</note>

# Arbatel of Magic

With the help of God, the truth will shine forth, according to the saying of Philo <note>Philo of Alexandria (c. 20 BCE – 50 CE), a Hellenistic Jewish philosopher who sought to harmonize Greek philosophy with Jewish Scripture.</note>.

### Aphorism 5
You shall love the Lord your God with all your heart, and with all your strength, and your neighbor as yourself. And the Lord will keep you as the apple of his eye, and will deliver you from all evil, and will replenish you with all good; and your soul shall desire nothing but that you shall be fully endowed with it, provided that it is consistent with the salvation of your soul and body.

### Aphorism 6
Whatever you have learned, repeat it frequently and fix it in your mind. Learn much, but do not try to learn too many different things at once, because a human understanding cannot be equally capable in all things—unless it is a mind that is divinely regenerated. To such a person, nothing is so difficult or complex that they may not be able to achieve it.

### Aphorism 7
> Call upon me in the day of trouble, and I will hear you, and
> you shall glorify me, says the Lord. For all ignorance is a
> tribulation <note>original: "tribulation of the minde"; a state of great trouble or suffering.</note> of the mind; therefore call upon the Lord in your
> ignorance, and he will hear you. And remember that you
> give honor unto God, and say with the Psalmist, *Not unto us,
> Lord, not unto us, but unto your Name give the glory.* <note>original: "Non nobis, Domine, non nobis, sed Nomini tuo da gloriam" (Psalm 115:1).</note>

# Arbatel of Magic

<margin>Aphorism 9.</margin>
God has distributed certain powers and offices from His treasures to things and persons. Therefore, the characters <note>original: "Characters"; refers to magical symbols or sigils</note> and names of stars do not have any power because of their shape or how they are pronounced. Instead, their power comes from the virtue or office which God has ordained by nature for such a name or character. For there is no power in heaven, on earth, or in hell which does not descend from God; and without His permission, these entities can neither give anything they possess nor be drawn into any action.

<margin>Aphorism 10.</margin>
The highest wisdom is that which comes from God. Next is the wisdom found in spiritual creatures; thirdly, in physical <note>original: "corporal"</note> creatures; fourthly, in Nature and natural things. The spirits who are apostate <note>rebellious or fallen angels</note> and reserved for the final judgment follow these after a long interval. Sixthly are the ministers of punishment in hell, who are obedient unto God. Seventhly, the Pygmies <note>In this context, Paracelsian nature spirits associated with the element of Earth; also known as Gnomes</note> and those who inhabit the elements and elementary things occupy the remaining places. 

It is necessary, therefore, to know and discern all the different levels of wisdom belonging to the Creator and the creatures. This allows it to be clearly manifest to us what we ought to take from every thing for our own use, and that we may truly know how and in what manner that should be done. For truly, every creature is ordained for some profitable end for human nature and for its service, as the Holy Scriptures, Reason, and Experience all testify.

God the Father Almighty, Creator of heaven and earth and of all things visible and invisible, declares in the Holy Scriptures that He keeps an eye over us. Like a tender father who loves his children, He teaches us what is profitable and what is not; what we are to avoid, and what we are to embrace. Then, He draws us toward obedience with great promises.

<summary>This page explains that magical power resides in God's appointment rather than symbols themselves and outlines a seven-fold hierarchy of wisdom ranging from God to elemental spirits.</summary>
<keywords>divine providence, sigils, hierarchy of spirits, Pygmies, elemental spirits, apostate spirits, spiritual wisdom, natural philosophy</keywords>

### Arbatel of Magic

...promises of physical and eternal benefits, and deters us (with threats of punishment) from those things which are not profitable for us. Turn over, therefore, with your hand—both night and day—those holy Writings, so that you may be happy in present things and blessed for all eternity. Do this, and you shall live according to what the holy Books have taught you.

### Aphorism II

The number four is Pythagorean <note>original: "Pythagorical"; referring to the teachings of Pythagoras, who viewed the number four (the Tetrad) as a symbol of justice and the foundation of the natural world</note> and the first square. Therefore, let us place here the foundation of all wisdom, following the wisdom of God revealed in the holy Scriptures and the observations proposed in Nature.

Grant, therefore, to the person who depends solely upon God, the wisdom of every creature to serve and obey him, whether they are willing or unwilling. In this, the omnipotence of God shines forth. This wisdom consists in our ability to distinguish the creatures that serve us from those that are unwilling, and that we may learn how to apply the wisdom and duties of every creature to ourselves. This Art is not handed down by human means, but divinely. To whom God wills, he reveals his secrets; but to those whom he will not bestow anything from his treasuries, that person shall attain nothing without the will of God.

Therefore, we ought truly to desire <gloss>to seek after spiritual things <note>original Greek: τὸ πνευματικὸν ἐπιζητεῖν</note></gloss> from God alone, who will mercifully grant these things to us. For he who has given us his Son and commanded us to pray for his holy Spirit, how much more will he subject to us the whole of creation, both things visible and invisible? *Whatever you ask, you shall receive.* Beware that you do not abuse the gifts of God, and all things shall work together for your salvation. And above all things, be watchful in this: that your names be written in Heaven. This is a greater matter than that the spirits should be obedient to you, as Christ admonishes.

<summary>This page establishes the spiritual foundation of magic, asserting that true power over creation comes only through divine will and the study of Scripture rather than human effort alone.</summary>
<keywords>Arbatel, Pythagorean number four, divine wisdom, spiritual gifts, obedience of spirits, providence, biblical authority</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of divine communication and the origins of magical arts, following the previous section on the nature of spiritual secrets.</meta>

### Aphorism 12.

In the Acts of the Apostles, the Spirit says to Peter after the Vision, *Go down, and do not doubt, for I have sent them*, when he was sent for by *Cornelius* the Centurion. In this same manner, through spoken words, all disciplines are delivered by the holy Angels of God, as is evident from the historical records <note>original: "Monuments"</note> of the Egyptians. These things were afterwards debased and corrupted by human opinions and by the instigation of evil spirits, who sow seeds of error <note>original: "tares"</note> among the children of disobedience, as is clear from the writings of *St. Paul* and *Hermes Trismegistus* <note>A legendary Hellenistic figure often identified as the founder of alchemy and hermetic philosophy.</note>. There is no other way of restoring these Arts than by the doctrine of the holy Spirits of God, because true faith comes by hearing. 

However, so that you may be certain of the truth and not doubt whether the spirits that speak with you declare things true or false, let your certainty depend only upon your faith in God. Thus, you may say with *Paul*, *I know in whom I have placed my trust*. If no sparrow can fall to the ground without the will of the Father who is in heaven, how much more will God protect you from being deceived—O you of little faith—if you depend wholly upon God and cling only to Him?

### Aphorism 13.

The Lord lives, and all things which live, do live in Him. And He is truly *Jehovah* <term>יהוה</term> <note>The Tetragrammaton, or the four-letter Hebrew name of God, representing the Eternal Being.</note>, who has given to all things the essence of what they are. By His word alone, through His Son, He has produced all things that exist out of nothing. He calls all the stars and all the host of heaven by their names. Therefore, that person knows the true strength and nature of things—and the order and governance <note>original: "policy"</note> of every creature, visible and invisible—to whom God has revealed the names of His creatures. It follows also that such a person receives power from God to extract the virtues in nature and the hidden secrets of the creature, and to bring forth their power...

<summary>This page argues that true spiritual knowledge is delivered by angels and must be verified through absolute faith in God to avoid the deceptions of evil spirits. it also asserts that understanding the divine names of creatures grants a practitioner the power to harness their natural and hidden virtues.</summary>
<keywords>Aphorisms, Peter the Apostle, Cornelius the Centurion, Hermes Trismegistus, divine revelation, Jehovah, Tetragrammaton, natural magic, angelic communication</keywords>

### Arbatel of Magic

Bring power into action, out of darkness into light. Your goal, therefore, ought to be to learn the names of the spirits—that is, their powers and offices—and how they are subjected and appointed by God to minister to you. This is just as *Raphael* was sent to *Tobias* <note>A reference to the Book of Tobit, where the angel Raphael heals Tobit’s blindness and protects his son Tobias.</note> so that he might heal his father, deliver his son from dangers, and bring him to a wife. So too *Michael*, the strength of God, governs the people of God; and *Gabriel*, the messenger of God, was sent to *Daniel*, *Mary*, and *Zachary*, the father of *John the Baptist*. He who will teach you whatever your soul desires regarding the nature of things shall be given to you if you desire him. You shall use his ministry with trembling and fear of your Creator, Redeemer, and Sanctifier—that is to say, the Father, Son, and Holy Ghost. Do not let any opportunity for learning slip away; be vigilant in your calling, and you will lack nothing that is necessary for you.

<margin>Deuteronomy 10; Aphorism 14.</margin>
Your soul lives forever through him who created you; therefore, call upon the Lord your God, and him only shall you serve. You will achieve this if you fulfill the purpose for which you were ordained by God, and perform what you owe to God and to your neighbor. God requires of you a mind that honors his Son and keeps the words of his Son in your heart. If you honor him, you have done the will of your Father who is in heaven. To your neighbor, you owe acts of humanity; you must encourage all men who come to you to honor the Son. This is the essence of the Law and the Prophets. In temporal <note>original: "temporal" — meaning worldly or physical as opposed to spiritual.</note> matters, you ought to call upon God as a father, asking that he give you all the necessities of this life. Furthermore, you ought to help your neighbor with the gifts which God bestows upon you, whether those gifts are spiritual or physical.

## Therefore you shall pray thus:

O Lord of Heaven and Earth, Creator and Maker of all things visible and invisible; I, though unworthy, call upon you by your assistance, through your only begotten Son Jesus Christ our Lord, that you will give to me your Holy Spirit, to direct me in your truth toward all good. Amen.

Because I earnestly desire to perfectly know the arts of this life, and such things as are necessary for us—which are so overwhelmed in darkness and polluted with infinite human opinions that I, by my own power, can attain no knowledge in them unless you teach me: Grant me, therefore, one of your spirits, who may teach me those things which you would have me to know and learn, to your praise and glory, and the benefit of our neighbor. Give me also an apt and teachable heart, that I may easily understand those things which you shall teach me, and may hide them in my understanding, so that I may bring them forth as if out of your inexhaustible treasures for all necessary uses. And give me grace, that I may use such of your gifts humbly, with fear and trembling, through our Lord Jesus Christ, with your Holy Spirit. Amen.

## The third Septenary <note>original: "Septenary"; a group or set of seven aphorisms or principles.</note>

### Aphorism 15.

They are called <term>Olympic spirits</term> <note>Spirits associated with the seven classical planets and the celestial spheres.</note>, who inhabit the firmament and the stars of the firmament. The office of these spirits is to declare <term>Destinies</term> and to administer <term>fatal charms</term> <note>original: "fatal Charms"; magical influences or decrees related to fate and the stars.</note>, so far as God pleases to permit them. For nothing—neither evil spirit nor evil destiny—shall be able to hurt him who has the Most High for his refuge. 

If, therefore, any of the <term>Olympic spirits</term> shall teach or declare that which the star to which he is appointed portends, nevertheless he can bring nothing into action unless he is permitted by the Divine power. It is God alone who gives them power to effect it. Unto God the maker of all things,

## Arbatel of Magic.

All things—celestial, earthly <note>original: "sublunary," meaning everything below the moon</note>, and infernal—are obedient to God. Therefore, rest in this: let God be your guide in all things which you undertake, and all things shall attain a happy and desired end; just as the history of the whole world testifies, and daily experience shows. "There is peace to the godly; there is no peace to the wicked," says the Lord.

<margin>Aphorism 16.</margin>
There are seven different governments of the Spirits of Olympus, by whom God has appointed the whole frame and universe of this world to be governed. Their visible stars are **Aratron, Bethor, Phaleg, Och, Hagith, Ophiel,** and **Phul**, according to the Olympic language. Every one of these has under him a mighty army <note>original: "Militia"</note> in the firmament.

**Aratron** rules 49 visible Provinces.
**Bethor** rules 32.
**Phaleg** rules 35.
**Och** rules 28.
**Hagith** rules 21.
**Ophiel** rules 14. <note>The OCR text lists XIII (13), but the mathematical total of 186 provided below suggests XIV (14) was intended.</note>
**Phul** rules 7.

So there are 186 Olympic Provinces in the whole Universe, wherein the seven Governors exercise their power; all of which are elegantly set forth in *Astronomy*. But in this place, it must be explained in what manner these Princes and Powers may be drawn into communication.

Aratron appears in the first hour of Saturday, and very truly gives answers concerning his Provinces and those who live within them. Likewise, the rest appear in order on their specific days and hours. Also, every one of them rules for 490 years. The beginning of their simple cycle <note>original: "Anomaly," referring here to a chronological period or astronomical cycle</note>, in the 60th year before the birth <note>original: "Nativity"</note> of Christ, was the beginning of the administration of Bethor; and it lasted until the year of our Lord 430. Phaleg succeeded him until the year 920. Then began,

<margin>Aphorism 17.</margin>
The rule of Bethor began in October and continued until the year 1410; from then on, Hagith rules until the year 1900.

Magically, the Princes of the seven Governors are called simply by their names and offices—which God has given to them—during the specific time, day, and hour in which they rule, whether visibly or invisibly. This is done by presenting the Character <note>A "Character" in this context is a sigil or mystical symbol used to summon or command a spirit.</note> which they have provided or confirmed.

The Governor **Aratron** has power over those things which he performs naturally; that is, in the same manner and subject as those things which in Astronomy <note>original: "Astronomy"; here referring to what we now call Astrology.</note> are attributed to the power of Saturn.

Those things which he performs of his own free will are:
1. He can instantly convert anything into stone, whether animal or plant, while keeping the object looking the same to the sight.
2. He converts treasures into coals, and coals into treasure.
3. He provides familiar spirits with a specific, defined power.
4. He teaches Alchemy, Magic, and Medicine <note>original: "Physick".</note>.
5. He reconciles subterranean spirits to men and creates "hairy men" <note>Likely referring to wood-woses or satyr-like spirits of the earth.</note>.
6. He enables a person to become invisible.
7. He makes the barren fruitful and grants long life.

His character:

#### The Most General Precepts of This Secret

1. Every Governor <note>The "Governors" are the seven Olympic Spirits who rule the world's regions</note> acts with all his spirits, either naturally—that is, always in the same manner—or otherwise of their own free will, provided God does not hinder them.

2. Every Governor is able to do all things that are naturally done over a long period of time using previously prepared matter; they can also do these things suddenly, using matter that has not been prepared. For example, Och, the Prince of solar things, produces gold in the mountains over a long time; he produces it in less time through the chemical art <note>alchemy</note>; and through magic, he produces it in a moment.

3. The true and divine magician may use all of God's creatures and the services of the Governors of the world at his own will. This is because the Governors of the world are obedient to such magicians; they come when they are called and execute...

execute their commands: but *God* is the author of it; just as *Joshua* caused the sun to stand still in the heavens. <note>Referring to Joshua 10:12-13, where God granted Joshua's command for the sun to stop.</note>

They send some of their spirits to mediocre magicians, who obey them only in specific, limited matters. However, they do not listen to false magicians; instead, by the command of *God*, they expose them to the deceits of devils and cast them into various dangers, as the prophet *Jeremiah* testifies in his eighth chapter concerning the Jews.

4. In all the elements, there are the seven governors with their hosts, who move in harmony with the motion of the firmament <note>The sphere of the stars and planets</note>; and the inferior beings always depend upon the superior ones, as is taught in philosophy.

5. A man who is a true magician is born a magician from his mother's womb. Others who devote themselves to this office without being called to it are unhappy. This is what *John the Baptist* speaks of: *No man can do anything of himself, except it be given him from above.* <note>original: "Nemo potest quicquam accipere, nisi fuerit ei datum de super." John 3:27.</note>

6. Every character <note>term: A magical seal, sigil, or symbolic signature</note> given by a spirit, for whatever cause, has its effectiveness for the specific task for which it was given, within the designated time. But it must be used on the same day and planetary hour in which it was given.

7. As God lives, and your soul lives: keep your covenant, and you shall have whatever the spirit reveals to you in God, because all things which the spirit promises to you shall be accomplished.

->*Aphorism 18*<-

There are other names of the Olympic spirits provided by other authors; but only those are effective which are delivered to an individual by the revealing spirit, whether visibly or invisibly. These names are delivered to each person according to how they are predestined; therefore they are called "Constellations," and they seldom remain effective for more than 40 years. Therefore, it is safest for young practitioners of the Art to work through the offices <note>term: The specific duties or functions of a spirit</note> of the spirits alone, without using their names. If they are pre-ordained to attain the Art of Magic, the other parts of the Art will offer themselves to them of their own accord. Pray...

### Aphorism 19.

Olympus and its inhabitants offer themselves to men of their own accord in the forms of Spirits; they are ready to perform their duties for them, whether they are requested or not. How much more readily will they attend to you, then, if they are desired? However, evil spirits and destroyers also appear; this is caused by the envy and malice of the devil, and because men draw these spirits to themselves through their sins, as a punishment due to sinners. Whoever, therefore, desires to have familiar conversation with Spirits, let him keep himself from all enormous sins and diligently pray to the Most High to be his protector. By doing so, he shall break through all the snares and obstacles of the devil. Let him apply himself to the service of God, and God will give him an increase in wisdom.

### Aphorism 20.

All things are possible to those who believe in them and are willing to receive them; but to the incredulous and unwilling, all things are impossible. There is no greater hindrance than a wavering mind, lightheadedness, inconstancy, foolish babbling, drunkenness, lusts, and disobedience to the word of God. A Magician, therefore, ought to be a man who is godly, honest, and constant in his words and deeds; he should have a firm faith toward God and be prudent, desiring nothing but wisdom concerning divine things.

### Aphorism 21.

When you wish to call any of the Olympic Spirits <note>The seven planetary governors of the universe according to this text.</note>, observe the rising of the Sun on 그 day, and consider the nature of the Spirit which you desire. By saying the following prayer, your desires shall be fulfilled:

[O Almighty...] <note>The text cuts off at "Omni-", the beginning of "Omnipotens," the Latin word for Almighty.</note>

### Arbatel of Magic

Omnipotent and eternal God, who has ordained the whole creation for your praise and glory, and for the salvation of man: I beseech you that you would send your Spirit *N. But you shall not detain the Spirit for more than a full hour, unless he is familiarly devoted to you.

Because you came in peace and quietly, and have answered my petitions, I give thanks unto God, in whose Name you came. And now you may depart in peace unto your orders; and return to me again when I shall call you by your names, or by your order, or by your office, which is granted from the Creator, Amen.

## The Third Septenary <note>A "Septenary" refers to a group of seven aphorisms or principles.</note>

Aphorism 22.
We call that a secret which no man can attain by human industry without revelation; this knowledge lies obscured and hidden by God within his creation. Nevertheless, God permits these secrets to be revealed by Spirits for the proper use of the thing itself. And these secrets concern things that are either divine, natural, or human. You may examine a few of the most select secrets, which you will then be able to use to discover many more.

### Aphorism 23.
Begin the discovery of a secret's nature either through a Spirit appearing in human form, or through separate virtues <note>spiritual powers or essences</note> acting through human organs, or by whatever other means this may be achieved. Once this is understood, ask a Spirit who is knowledgeable in that specific art to briefly declare the essence of that secret to you. Pray to God that he would inspire you with his grace, so that you may bring the secret to the goal you desire, for the praise and glory of God and the benefit of your neighbor.

### Aphorism 24.
->The greatest secrets are seven in number.<-

1. The first is the curing of all diseases within the span of seven days, whether by characters <note>talismanic symbols or sigils</note>, by natural substances, or by the higher Spirits with divine assistance.
2. The second is to be able to prolong life to whatever age we please—specifically, a physical and natural life.
3. The third is to have the obedience of the creatures inhabiting the elements, who appear in the forms of personal Spirits; these include Pygmies <note>original: "Pigrnies"; earth spirits or gnomes</note>, Sagani <note>a term used by Paracelsus for elemental spirits</note>, Nymphs, Dryads, and Spirits of the Woods.

<margin>Spirits of the four elements. Paracelsus.</margin>

4. The fourth is to be able to speak with knowledge and understanding of all things visible and invisible, and to understand the power of every thing and its proper purpose.
5. The fifth is that a man be able to govern himself according to the purpose for which God has appointed him.
6. The sixth is to know God, Christ, and his Holy Spirit; this is the perfection of the Microcosm <note>original: "Microcosmus"; the concept of man as a "little world" reflecting the universe</note>.
7. The seventh is to be regenerate <note>spiritually reborn</note>, like Enoch <note>original: "Henochius"</note>, the King of the inferior world.

A man of an honest and constant mind may learn these seven secrets from the Spirits without any offense toward God.

# Arbatel of Magic.
## The intermediate secrets are likewise seven in number.

1. The first is the transmutation of metals, which is commonly called *Alchemy*; which certainly is given to very few, and only by special grace.
2. The second is the curing of diseases with metals, either by the magnetic virtues of precious stones, or by the use of the Philosopher's Stone <note>A legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and providing the elixir of life.</note>, and similar means.
3. The third is to be able to perform astronomical and mathematical miracles, such as *hydraulic* engines <note>Machines powered by water pressure, often used in the Renaissance for automata or fountains.</note>, to manage affairs by the influence of Heaven, and other things of a similar sort.
4. The fourth is to perform the works of natural magic, of whatever kind they may be.
5. The fifth is to know all physical secrets <note>In this context, "physical" refers to the study of nature and medicine.</note>.
6. The sixth is to know the foundation of all arts which are practiced with the hands and the functions of the body.
7. The seventh is to know the foundation of all arts which are practiced by the angelic nature of man <note>Refers to the intellectual and spiritual faculties of humans that mirror the nature of angels.</note>.

## The lesser secrets are seven.

1. The first is to do things diligently and to accumulate much money.
2. The second is to rise from a humble state to positions of dignity and honor, and to establish a new family line which may be illustrious and achieve great things.
3. The third is to excel in military affairs, to successfully achieve great things, and to be a leader among the leaders of Kings and Princes.
4. The fourth is to be a good householder <note>original: "house-keeper"</note> both in the country and the city.
5. The fifth is to be an industrious and fortunate merchant.
6. The sixth is to be a philosopher, mathematician, and physician, according to the teachings of *Aristotle, Plato, Ptolemy, Euclid, Hippocrates,* and *Galen*.
7. To...

7. To be a theologian <note>original: "Divine"</note> according to the Bible and the Schools <note>The "Schools" refers to Scholasticism, the traditional system of theology and philosophy taught in medieval European universities.</note>, which all writers of divinity, both old and new, have taught.

<margin>Aphorism 25.</margin> We have already declared what a secret is, as well as its types and species; it remains now to show how we may attain the knowledge of those things which we desire.

The true and only way to all secrets is to have recourse unto God, the Author of all good; and as Christ teaches: *In the first place, seek the kingdom of God and his righteousness, and all these things shall be added unto you.*

1. Also, see that your hearts are not burdened with overindulgence <note>original: "surfeiting"</note>, drunkenness, and the cares of this life.
3. Also, commit your cares unto the Lord, and he will do it.
4. Also, *I the Lord your God teach you what things are profitable for you, and guide you in the way wherein you walk.*
5. *And I will give you understanding, and will teach you in the way wherein you shall go, and I will guide you with my eye.*
6. Also, *if you who are evil know how to give good things to your children, how much more shall your Father who is in heaven give his Holy Spirit to those who ask him?*
7. *If you will do the will of my Father who is in heaven, you are truly my disciples, and we will come unto you and make our abode with you.*

If you draw these seven passages of Scripture from the literal letter into the Spirit—or into action—you cannot err, but shall attain the desired goal. You shall not miss the mark, and God himself, by his Holy Spirit, will teach you true and profitable things. He will also give his ministering angels unto you to be your companions, helpers, and teachers of all the secrets of the world; and he will command every creature to be obedient unto you, so that, cheerfully rejoicing, you may say with the Apostles: *That the spirits are obedient unto you.* Thus, at length, you shall be certain of the greatest thing of all: *That your name is written in Heaven.*

## Arbatel of Magic.

### Aphorism 26.
There is another way, which is more common, by which secrets may be revealed to you even when you are unaware of it. This happens either through God, or by spirits who have secrets in their power; or through dreams, or by strong imaginations and impressions, or by the constellation of one's birth <note>original: "nativity"</note> through celestial knowledge. In this manner, "heroic" men are made—such as there are very many—including all the learned men in the world: *Plato, Aristotle, Hippocrates, Galen, Euclid, Archimedes, Hermes Trismegistus* (the father of secrets), along with *Theophrastus Paracelsus*. All of these men possessed within themselves all the powers <note>original: "vertues"</note> of secrets. To this category we also refer *Homer, Hesiod, Orpheus,* and *Pythagoras*; however, these did not possess such great gifts of secrets as the former group. To this are also referred the Nymphs, the sons of *Melusina* <note>A legendary figure of European folklore, often depicted as a woman who is a serpent or fish from the waist down.</note>, and the Gods of the Pagans <note>original: "Gentiles"</note>, such as *Achilles, Aeneas,* and *Hercules*; also *Cyrus, Alexander the Great, Julius Caesar, Lucullus, Sulla,* and *Marius*.

It is a Rule <note>original: "Canon"</note> that everyone should know his own Angel and obey him according to the Word of God. Let him beware of the snares of the evil Angel, lest he be caught up in the calamities that befell *Brutus* and *Mark Antony*. On this subject, refer to the book by *Jovianus Pontanus* regarding Fortune, and his work *Eutyches*.

The third way is diligent and hard labor. Without this, no great thing worthy of admiration can be obtained from the Divine Deity, as it is said:

->*You shall say or do nothing against the will of Minerva.* <note>original Latin: "Tu nihil invita dices faciésve Minerva." A classical proverb meaning one should not act against one's natural talent or divine inspiration.</note><-

We utterly reject <note>original: "detest"</note> all evil magicians who make themselves associates with devils through their unlawful superstitions. They obtain and accomplish certain things which God permits to be done as a form of punishment by the devils. They also perform other evil acts with the devil as the author, as the Scriptures testify.

->Aphorism 27<-

Make a circle with a center A, which is B, C, D, E. At the East, let there be B. C. a square. At the North, C. D. At the West, D. E. And at the South, E. D. Divide the several quadrants into seven parts, so that there may be 28 parts in the whole; and let them be again divided into four parts, so that there may be 112 parts of the circle; and so many are the true secrets to be revealed. 

And this circle, divided in this manner, is the seal of the secrets of the world, which they draw from the only center A—that is, from the invisible God—unto the whole of creation. The Prince of the Oriental <note>Eastern</note> secrets is resident in the middle and has three Nobles on either side, every one of whom has four under him, and the Prince himself has four appertaining unto him. 

In this manner, the other princes and nobles have their quadrants of secrets, each with their four secrets. But the Oriental secret is the study of all wisdom; the West, of strength; the South, of tillage <note>agriculture or cultivation</note>; the North, of a more rigid life. So it is that the Eastern secrets are commended as the best; the Meridian <note>Southern</note> to be average; and the West and North to be lesser. 

The use of this seal of secrets is that you may know from where the spirits or angels are produced, who may teach the secrets delivered unto them from God. But they have names taken from their offices and powers, according to the gift which God has severally distributed to every one of them. One has the power of the sword; another, of the pestilence; and another, of inflicting famine upon the people, as it is ordained by God. Some are destroyers of cities, as those two were who were sent to overthrow *Sodom* and *Gomorrah* and the places adjacent, examples of which the holy...

## Arbatel of Magic

As the Holy Scripture witnesses, some are the watchmen over kingdoms, while others are the keepers of private persons. From this, anyone may easily form their names in his own language. Thus, whoever wishes may ask for a physical, mathematical, or philosophical Angel, or an Angel of civil wisdom, or of supernatural or natural wisdom, or for anything whatsoever. Let him ask seriously, with a great desire of his mind, and with faith and constancy; and without doubt, he shall receive what he asks for from the Father and God of all spirits. 

This faith surmounts all seals <note>original: "seals" refers to sigils or symbolic diagrams used in ritual magic to command spirits</note> and brings them into subjection to the will of man. The characteristic manner of calling Angels follows this faith, which depends solely on divine revelation; but without the aforementioned faith preceding it, it lies in obscurity. Nevertheless, if anyone will use them as a memorial <note>original: "memorial" here means a mnemonic device or a physical reminder of a spiritual truth</note>, and not otherwise, and as a thing simply created by God for this purpose—to which such a spiritual power or

## The fifth Septenary.

Aphorism 29.
As our study of Magic proceeds in order from the general rules previously mentioned, let us now come to a particular explanation of it. Spirits are either divine ministers of the Word, of the Church, and its members; or else they are servants to the Creatures in physical things—partly for the salvation of the soul and body, and partly for its destruction. And there is nothing done, whether good or evil, without a certain and determined order and government. He who seeks after a good end, let him follow it; and he who desires an evil end, let him pursue that also, and do so earnestly, resulting in divine punishment and a turning away from the divine will. Therefore, let everyone compare his goals with the Word of God, using it as a touchstone <note>term: touchstone; a standard or criterion by which something is judged or recognized</note> that will judge between good and evil. Let him propose to himself what is to be avoided and what is to be sought after; and that which he establishes and determines for himself, let him follow diligently, not procrastinating or delaying, until he attains his appointed goal.

### Aphorism 30.

Those who desire riches, worldly glory, magistracy <note>term: magistracy; the office or power of a public official or administrator</note>, honors, ranks, or power (attained through magic), will obtain them if they strive diligently for them. Each shall receive according to their destiny, industry, and knowledge of the magical sciences. This is demonstrated by the history of Melusine <note>original: "Melesina"; a legendary figure of European folklore, sometimes associated with the founding of noble houses</note> and her magicians, who decreed that no one of the Italian nation should ever possess the rule or Kingdom of Naples. They brought this to pass, causing the ruler of that age to be thrown down from his throne. Such is the great power of the guardian or tutelary <note>term: tutelary; serving as a protector, guardian, or patron</note> Angels of the kingdoms of the world.

### Aphorism 31.

Call upon the Prince of the Kingdom and issue a command to him, and whatever you command shall be done—provided that Prince is not released from his obedience by a succeeding magician. Therefore, the Kingdom of Naples could be restored to the Italians if any magician were to call upon the one who first established this order and compel him to undo his deed. He might also be compelled to restore the secret power taken from the treasury of magic: a Book, a Gem, and a magical Horn. If someone possessed these, they could easily, if they wished, make themselves the monarch of the world. However, "the Jew" <note>original: "Judæus"; likely referring to a specific legendary figure or a personification of Jewish mystical tradition in this context</note> chose instead to live among the gods until the final judgment, preferring that over the fleeting goods of this world. Yet his heart is so blind that he understands nothing of the God of heaven and earth, or thinks of nothing more; instead, he enjoys the pleasures of immortal things to his own eternal destruction. He can be summoned more easily than the guardian spirit <note>original: "Angel"</note> of Plotinus in the Temple of Isis <note>note: This refers to a historical anecdote where an Egyptian priest summoned the philosopher Plotinus's guardian spirit, which appeared as a god rather than a lesser spirit</note>.

### Aphorism 32.

In the same way, the Romans were taught by the Sibylline Books <note>note: A collection of oracular prophecies consulted by the Roman state during emergencies</note>; by that means, they made themselves the lords of the world, as historical records witness. However, the lords who serve the Prince of a Kingdom are the ones who bestow the lesser offices <note>original: "Magistracies"</note>. Therefore, anyone who desires to have a lesser office or rank, let him—

### Aphorism 33.

But he who covets lowly honors <note>original: "contemptible dignities"</note>, such as riches alone, let him call the Prince of Riches or one of his Lords, and he shall obtain his desire in whatever way he wishes to grow wealthy. This might be through earthly goods, trade, the gifts of Princes, or through the study of metals and alchemy <note>original: "Chymistry"; in this era, chemistry and alchemy were often considered the same pursuit of transforming materials and understanding nature</note>. Provided he follows an established method <note>original: "president" (precedent)</note> for growing rich by these means, he shall achieve his desire.

### Aphorism 34.

All forms of summoning <note>original: "evocation"</note> follow the same kind and form. This method was familiar in ancient times to the Sibyls <note>term: Sibyls; female prophets of the ancient world believed to be inspired by gods</note> and the high priests. In our own time, through ignorance and a lack of religious devotion, this knowledge is almost entirely lost; what little remains is corrupted by infinite lies and superstitions.

### Aphorism 35.

The human understanding is the only agent of all wonderful works, provided it is joined to a Spirit; once joined, the mind produces whatever it desires. Therefore, we must be careful how we proceed in magic, lest sirens <note>original: "Syrens"; mythological creatures used here as a metaphor for deceptive or alluring spirits</note> and other monsters deceive us, for they also desire the company of the human soul. Let the magician always hide himself under the wings of the Most High, so that he does not offer himself to be devoured by the "roaring lion" <note>A biblical reference to 1 Peter 5:8, describing the devil as a lion seeking someone to devour</note>. For those who desire earthly things can only with great difficulty escape the snares of the devil.

...appointed end. For example, those who perform cures using the most simple herbs and roots achieve the most successful results of all. In this same way, the greatest influences or virtues actually lie hidden within constellations, words, symbols <note>original: "Characters"</note>, stones, and similar things, which act as miracles.

The same is true of words: when they are spoken, they immediately cause both visible and invisible creatures to obey. This applies to creatures of our own world as well as those of the water, air, underground, and Olympic <note>term: Olympic; in this context, referring to the planetary or celestial spirits associated with the heavens</note>, super-celestial, infernal, and even divine realms.

Therefore, simplicity is what should be studied most of all, and the knowledge of such medicinal plants <note>term: simples; herbal medicines made from a single ingredient</note> must be sought from God; otherwise, they cannot be discovered by any other means or experience.

->*Aphorism 37.*<-

Let everything be handled properly <note>original: "let all lots have their place decently"</note>: Order, Reason, and Means are the three things that make all learning—regarding both visible and invisible creatures—easy to achieve. 

This is how Order works: some creatures belong to the light, while others belong to darkness. The latter are subject to vanity because they rush headlong into darkness and enslave themselves in eternal punishment because of their rebellion. Their kingdom is, on one hand, very beautiful in fleeting and perishable <note>original: "corruptible"</note> things, because it cannot exist without some virtue and the great gifts of God. On the other hand, it is most filthy and horrible to describe because it overflows with all wickedness and sin: idolatry, contempt for God, blasphemies against the true God and His works, the worship of devils, disobedience toward authorities <note>original: "Magistrates"</note>, rebellions, murders, robberies, tyranny, adulteries, wicked lusts, rapes, thefts, lies, perjuries, pride, and a greedy desire for power. The kingdom of darkness consists of this mixture. 

However, the creatures of light are filled with eternal truth and the grace of God; they are lords of the whole world and reign over the lords of darkness as the members of Christ. Between these two groups, there is a continual war until God ends their conflict with His final judgment.

<summary>This page explains that the most powerful magic is found in nature's simplest forms (herbs, words, and stones) and outlines the eternal conflict between the virtuous kingdom of light and the sinful kingdom of darkness.</summary>
<keywords>simples, medicinal herbs, Olympic spirits, kingdom of darkness, kingdom of light, cosmic order, spiritual warfare, Arbatel</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the classification of magic, shifting from the general principles of order and simplicity to a detailed sevenfold division of how magic is categorized based on its source, its tools, and the types of spirits involved.</meta>

<page-num>196</page-num>
<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

->Aphorism 38.<-

Therefore, Magic is divided into two primary parts: the first is from God, which He bestows upon the creatures of light; the other is also from God, but it is the gift which He gives to the creatures of darkness. This second type is also twofold: one is for a good purpose, such as when the Princes of darkness are compelled by God's force to do good for His creatures; the other is for an evil purpose, when God allows such spirits to punish wicked people so that they are magically deceived into their own destruction, or when He commands such spirits to be cast out into ruin.

The second division of Magic is based on its methods: it accomplishes some works using visible instruments and physical things; it effects other works with invisible instruments and spiritual means; and it performs other tasks using a mixture of both visible and invisible means, instruments, and effects.

The third division concerns the source of power: there are some things brought to pass by the invocation of God alone. This is partly Prophetic, partly Philosophical, and partly what might be called "Theophrastical" <note>term: Theophrastical; referring to divine wisdom or the knowledge of God's direct influence on nature, a term often associated with Paracelsus</note>. 

Other things are done through the "Princes of Spirits" <note>term: Princes of Spirits; high-ranking spiritual entities or demons</note> because of an ignorance of the true God, so that the practitioner's desires may be fulfilled; such is the work of the Mercurialists <note>term: Mercurialists; those who practice magic related to the planetary spirit of Mercury, often associated with intellect, communication, and swift results</note>.

The fourth division is that some practice their Magic with good Angels instead of God, as if descending down from the Most High God; such was the Magic of the Baalim <note>original: "Baalim"; likely referring here to ancient practitioners who worked through celestial intermediaries or planetary lords</note>.

Another type of Magic is that which carries out its actions through the leaders of evil spirits; such were those who worked through the "minor gods" of the heathens.

The fifth division is that some interact with spirits openly and face-to-face, which is a gift granted to very few. Others work through dreams and other signs, which the ancients derived from their auguries <note>term: auguries; the practice of interpreting omens from the flight of birds or other natural signs</note> and sacrifices.

The sixth division is that some work with immortal creatures, while others work with mortal creatures such as Nymphs, Satyrs, and similar inhabitants of the other elements, like Pygmies <note>term: Pigmies; in Renaissance magic, this refers to Gnomes or elemental spirits of the Earth, not the ethnic groups of the same name</note>, and so on.

The seventh division is that the spirits serve some of
<meta>catchword: their</meta>

<keywords>Aphorism 38, types of magic, creatures of light, creatures of darkness, Theophrastical, Mercurialists, elementals, Nymphs, Satyrs, Pigmies</keywords>

of their own accord, without art; others they will hardly attend to, even when called by art.

Among these types of Magic, that which is the most excellent of all is the one which depends upon God alone. The second is that in which the spirits serve faithfully of their own accord. The third is that which is the property of Christians, which depends on the power of Christ which he has in heaven and earth.

### Aphorism 39.

*There is a seven-fold preparation to learn the Magic Art.*

The first is to meditate day and night on how to attain the true knowledge of God, both by His word revealed from the foundation of the world, and also by the "seal of creation" <note>term: seal of creation; the idea that God's nature or "signature" is imprinted upon the physical world and its creatures</note> and the creatures themselves, and by the wonderful effects which the visible and invisible creatures of God display.

Secondly, it is necessary that a person look deep within themselves and chiefly study to know themselves: what mortal part they have within them, what immortal part, and what part is unique to themselves and what is shared with others.

Thirdly, that they learn by their own immortal part to worship, love, and fear the eternal God, and to adore him in Spirit and Truth; and with their mortal part, to do those things which they know to be acceptable to God and profitable to their neighbors.

These are the first three and most important precepts of Magic. Let everyone who desires to obtain true Magic or divine wisdom prepare themselves with these, so that they may be considered worthy of it—as one whom the angelic creatures willingly serve, not only in secret <note>original: "occultly"</note> but also openly <note>original: "manifestly"</note>, and as it were face to face.

Fourthly, whereas every person is watchful to see to what kind of life they shall be called from their mother's womb, everyone should know whether they are born for Magic, and to what type of it. This is something anyone may easily perceive who...

<sig>Dd 2</sig>

<summary>This page outlines the highest forms of magic—specifically that which comes from God or through Christ—and begins a list of seven preparations for a magician, focusing on the knowledge of God, self-knowledge, and moral duty.</summary>
<keywords>divine magic, Christian magic, preparation for magic, self-knowledge, angelic service, Aphorism 39, divine wisdom</keywords>

--- Page 218 ---
<meta>This page continues the "seven-fold preparation" for learning the Magic Art, concluding the final three steps of preparation from Aphorism 39 and beginning Aphorism 40, which outlines the laws for governing spiritual operations.</meta>

<page-num>198</page-num>
<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

...reads these things and through experience may find success in them; for such things and such gifts are not given to any but the lowly and humble.

Fifth, we must take care to understand when the spirits are assisting us in undertaking the greatest business. It is clear that whoever understands this shall be made a Magician by God's appointment <note>original: "ordination of God"</note>; that is, a person who uses the ministry of spirits to achieve excellent things. In this area, most people sin through negligence, ignorance, contempt, or excessive superstition. They also offend God through ingratitude—an error for which many famous men have later brought destruction upon themselves. They also sin through rashness and obstinacy, or when they do not use their gifts for the honor of God as required, and instead prefer secondary matters to essential work <note>original: *πάρεργα ἔργοις*</note>.

Sixth, the Magician needs faith and discretion <note>original: "taciturnity"</note>, especially so that he does not disclose any secret that the Spirit has forbidden him to share. Just as God commanded *Daniel* to seal certain things—meaning, not to declare them in public—so it was not lawful for *Paul* to speak openly of all the things he saw in a vision. No one will believe how much wisdom is contained in this single rule.

Seventh, in one who would be a Magician, the greatest justice is required. He must undertake nothing that is ungodly, wicked, or unjust, nor even let such things enter his mind; in this way, he shall be divinely protected from all evil.

### Aphorism 40.

When the Magician determines within himself to perform any spiritual <note>original: "incorporeal"</note> act, whether through external or internal senses, let him then govern himself according to these seven subsequent laws to accomplish his magical goal.

The first law is this: he must know that such a Spirit is assigned to him by God, and he should reflect that God is the observer of all his thoughts and actions. Therefore, let him direct the entire course of his life according to the rules prescribed in the word of God.

<meta>catchword: Secondly,</meta>

<meta>This page continues the rules for spiritual operations within Aphorism 40, listing the second through seventh precautions for a practitioner, before transitioning to Aphorism 41, which serves as the introduction to the final section of the book.</meta>

<page-num>199</page-num>

<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

Secondly, always pray with *David*:

> "Take not your holy Spirit from me; and strengthen me with your free Spirit; and lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil. I beg you, O heavenly Father, do not give power over me to any lying spirit, as you did over *Ahab* so that he perished; but keep me in your truth. Amen." <note>This prayer combines elements of Psalm 51 and the Lord's Prayer with a specific reference to the biblical King Ahab, who was led to his death by a "lying spirit" (1 Kings 22).</note>

Thirdly, let the practitioner accustom himself to testing the spirits, as the *Scripture* advises <note>original: "admonisheth"</note>; for grapes cannot be gathered from thorns. Let us test all things and hold fast to that which is good and praiseworthy, so that we may avoid everything that is contrary to divine power.

Fourthly, be far removed and clear from all kinds of superstition. It is superstition to attribute divinity to things that contain nothing divine, or to choose or create for ourselves ways of worshipping God that he has not commanded. Such are the magical ceremonies of Satan, through which he shamelessly <note>original: "impudently"</note> offers himself to be worshipped as God.

Fifthly, avoid <note>original: "eschewed"</note> all worship of idols, which attempts to bind divine power to statues or other objects by their own impulse <note>original: "proper motion"</note>, where they have not been placed by the Creator or the order of Nature. Many false and wicked magicians invent <note>original: "faign"</note> such things.

Sixthly, all the deceitful imitations and affects of the devil are also to be avoided. He tries to imitate the power of creation and the Creator, attempting to produce things with a word so that they appear to be what they are not. This power belongs only to the omnipotence of God and cannot be shared with any created being <note>original: "creature"</note>.

Seventhly, let us cling <note>original: "cleave fast"</note> to the gifts of God and his Holy Spirit, so that we may know them and diligently embrace them with our whole heart and all our strength.

### Aphorism 41.

We come now to the final nine aphorisms of this entire volume <note>original: "Tome"</note>; with these, by the help of divine mercy, we will conclude this whole *Magical Isagoge* <note>term: Isagoge: An introductory manual or treatise, from the Greek 'eisagoge'.</note>.

Therefore, in the first place, it should be noted what we mean by the term "Magician" in this work.

<meta>This page continues the definition of magic and the requirements for a practitioner, building on the precautions listed in Aphorism 41. It defines the distinction between a true magician and an evil one, and introduces Aphorism 42 regarding the divine calling of a magician.</meta>

<header>Arbatel of Magic.</header>

We therefore consider a person to be a Magician if, by the grace of God, the spiritual essences <note>original: "spiritual essences"; refers to spiritual beings or entities like angels or spirits</note> serve them to reveal the knowledge of the entire universe and the secrets of Nature contained within it, whether they are visible or invisible. This description of a Magician is clearly evident and universal.

An evil Magician is one whom evil spirits serve—by divine permission—to his own earthly <note>original: "temporal"</note> and eternal destruction and ruin, to deceive people and draw them away from God. Such a person was *Simon Magus*, who is mentioned in the *Acts of the Apostles* and in the writings of *Clement* <note>original: "Clemens"; refers to Clement of Rome, the traditional author of the "Clementine" literature which details the conflicts between St. Peter and Simon Magus</note>. *St. Peter* commanded him to be cast down to the earth after Simon had ordered himself to be lifted into the air by unclean spirits as if he were a god.

To this category we should also refer all those who are noted in the two Tables of the Law <note>The Ten Commandments</note> and are described along with their evil deeds.

We will describe the subdivisions and types of both kinds of Magic in the following volumes <note>original: "Tomes"</note>. For now, it is enough that we distinguish between the sciences, identifying which is good and which is evil. Humanity sought to obtain both at the beginning to their own ruin and destruction, as *Moses* and *Hermes* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary figure to whom the Hermetic writings are attributed</note> demonstrate.

### Aphorism 42.

Secondly, we must know that a magician is a person predestined <note>original: "predestinated"</note> to this work from his mother's womb. No one should take such great things upon themselves unless they are divinely called by grace for some good purpose. A calling for a bad purpose exists so that the Scripture might be fulfilled: *“It is inevitable that stumbling blocks <note>original: "offences"</note> will come, but woe to that man through whom they come.”* <note>Matthew 18:7</note> Therefore, as we have often warned before, we must live in this world with fear and trembling.

Nevertheless, I will not deny that some people may obtain certain types of both kinds of Magic through study and diligence, if it is permitted. However, such a person will never reach the highest forms of magic. If they long to attempt to master <note>original: "assail"</note> them, they will undoubtedly cause harm to both their soul and

<meta>The text cuts off mid-sentence, continuing with "and body" on the next page.</meta>

Such are they who, through the operations of false magicians, are sometimes carried to Mount *Horeb*, or into some wilderness or deserts; or they are maimed in some limb <note>original: "member"</note>, or are simply torn to pieces, or are deprived of their understanding. Many such things happen through the use of this magic where people are forsaken by God and delivered to the power of Satan.

## The Seventh Septenary <note>A "septenary" refers to a group of seven; the Arbatel is organized into sets of seven aphorisms.</note>

### Aphorism 43.

The Lord lives, and the works of God live in Him by His appointment, through which He wills them to exist. For He intends for them to use their liberty in obedience to His commands, or in disobedience to them. To the obedient, He has proposed their rewards; to the disobedient, He has set forth their deserved punishment. 

Therefore these spirits, through their free will, pride, and contempt for the Son of God, have revolted from God their Creator and are reserved for the day of wrath. A very great power remains in them over creation; nonetheless, it is limited, and they are confined to their boundaries by the bridle <note>A metaphor for restraint or control</note> of God. 

Therefore, the **Magician of God**—which signifies a wise person of God, or one informed by God—is led forth by the hand of God toward all everlasting good, including both ordinary <note>original: "mean"</note> matters and also the most important physical <note>original: "corporal"</note> things.

The power of Satan is great because of the great sins of humanity. Therefore, the magicians of Satan also perform great things—greater than any person would believe. Although they remain within their own limits, they are nevertheless above all human comprehension regarding the physical and fleeting <note>original: "transitory"</note> things of this life, as many ancient histories and daily examples testify. Both kinds of Magic are different from one another in their goals: one leads to eternal good and uses temporary things with thanksgiving; the

the other is little concerned <note>original: "sollicitous"</note> about eternal things, but wholly exercises himself in physical <note>original: "corporal"</note> things, so that he may freely enjoy all his lusts and delights in contempt of God and His anger.

### *Aphorism 44.*

The transition from the ordinary life of man to a magical life is nothing other than a sleep from that life and an awakening to this life; for those things which happen to ignorant and unwise men in their ordinary life, the same things happen to the willing and knowledgeable magician.

The magician understands when the mind meditates on its own; he deliberates, reasons, establishes, and determines what is to be done. He observes when his thoughts <note>original: "cogitations"</note> proceed from a divine separate essence <note>A spirit or entity distinct from the human mind</note>, and he examines <note>original: "proveth"</note> what order that divine separate essence belongs to.

But the person who is ignorant of magic is tossed to and fro, as if at war with their own emotions <note>original: "affections"</note>. They do not know when these feelings emerge from their own mind or are impressed upon them by an assisting essence; and they do not know how to overthrow the plans <note>original: "counsels"</note> of their enemies by the Word of God, or how to keep themselves from the snares and deceits of the tempter <note>Satan or demonic influences</note>.

### *Aphorism 45.*

The greatest rule <note>original: "precept"</note> of magic is to know what every person ought to receive for their use from the assisting spirit, and what to refuse. One may learn this from the Psalmist, who says: *How shall a young man cleanse his way? By keeping your word, O Lord.* <note>Psalm 119:9</note> To keep the Word of God, so that the evil one does not snatch it out of the heart, is the chief rule of wisdom. 

It is lawful to accept and act upon other suggestions which are not contrary to the glory of God and charity toward our neighbors, without inquiring from which spirit such suggestions proceed. But we must take care that we are not too preoccupied with unnecessary things, according to the warning <note>original: "admonition"</note> of Christ: *Martha, Martha, you are troubled about many things; but Mary has chosen the better part, which shall...* <note>Luke 10:41-42. The catchword "not" suggests the next page continues "not be taken away from her."</note>

<summary>This page defines the "magical life" as a state of spiritual awakening and discernment, where the magician learns to distinguish between their own thoughts and the influence of external spirits. It emphasizes that the core of magical wisdom lies in adhering to the Word of God and focusing on spiritual priorities over worldly distractions.</summary>

not be taken from her. Therefore, let us always have regard for the saying of Christ: *Seek first the Kingdom of God and His righteousness, and all these things shall be added to you.* All other things—that is, all things which are due to the mortal microcosm <note>original: "Microcosme"; the Renaissance idea that a human being is a "little world" reflecting the entire universe</note>, such as food, clothing <note>original: "raiment"</note>, and the necessary arts of this life.

### Aphorism 46.

Nothing becomes a person as much as constancy in their words and deeds. When like rejoices in like, none are happier than such people, because the holy angels dwell among them and keep watch over them. On the contrary, inconstant people are lighter than nothing, like rotten leaves. We choose the 46th Aphorism from these principles: Just as every person governs themselves, so they attract to themselves spirits of their own nature and condition. However, one truly advises that no one should carry themselves beyond their own calling <note>one's social or spiritual station in life</note>, lest they draw to themselves some evil <note>original: "malignant"</note> spirit from the ends of the earth, by whom they shall either be made foolish and deceived, or led to final destruction.

This rule <note>original: "precept"</note> appears most plainly: for Midas, when he wished to convert all things into gold, drew such a spirit to himself that was able to perform this; and being deceived by it, he would have been brought to death by famine if his foolishness had not been corrected by the mercy of God. The same thing happened in our times to a certain woman near Frankfurt on the Oder <note>original: "Fanckford at Odera"</note>, who sought to scrape together and consume money from anything. I wish that people would diligently weigh this rule and not consider the stories of Midas and similar accounts as mere fables; they would be much more diligent in moderating their thoughts and affections, and they would not be so perpetually troubled by the spirits of the "golden mountains of Utopia" <note>A reference to Thomas More’s *Utopia*; here meaning impossible or illusory wealth</note>. Therefore, we ought most diligently to observe that such arrogant thoughts <note>original: "presumptions"</note> should be cast out of the mind by the Word of God while they are new; do not let them become a habit in an idle mind that is empty of the divine Word.

Using the examples of Midas and a greedy woman from Frankfurt, the author advises keeping the mind filled with the Word of God to avoid the snares of illusory wealth.</summary>
