### Aphorism 47.

Whoever is faithfully dedicated to their calling <note>original: "vocation"</note> will also have spirits as constant companions to their desires, and they will continually provide for them in all things. But if they have any knowledge of magic, the spirits will not be unwilling to reveal themselves, to converse familiarly, and to serve in the various ministries to which they are assigned; good spirits assist in good matters leading to salvation, while evil spirits assist in every evil thing leading to destruction. Examples of this are plentiful throughout world history and occur daily in the world. *Theodosius*, before his victory over *Arbogastus*, is an example of the good <note>The Roman Emperor Theodosius I defeated Arbogastus at the Battle of the Frigidus in 394 AD, a victory attributed by Christian historians to divine intervention and miraculous winds</note>. *Brutus*, before he was killed, was an example of the evil spirits, when he was haunted by the ghost <note>original: "Spirit"</note> of *Caesar* and driven to punishment, so that he killed himself—he who had slain his own "father" and the Father of his Country <note>Marcus Junius Brutus was a lead assassin of Julius Caesar; Caesar had been a mentor and was rumored to be his biological father, and held the title "Pater Patriae" or Father of the Country</note>.

### Aphorism 48.

All magic is a revelation from spirits that correspond to the type of magic being practiced. In the works of *Hesiod*, the nine *Muses* are referred to as "the ninth Magic," as he clearly testifies of himself in his *Theogony* <note>original: "Theogony"; a poem by Hesiod describing the origins and genealogies of the Greek gods</note>. In *Homer*, the guardian spirit <note>original: "genius"</note> of *Ulysses* is seen in the *Psychagogia* <note>original: "Psigioga-gia"; the calling up of spirits, specifically referring to the "Nekyia" in Book 11 of the Odyssey</note>. *Hermes* <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary author of the Hermetic corpus</note> speaks of the spirits of the more sublime parts of the mind. God revealed himself to *Moses* in the burning bush. The three wise men who came to seek Christ at *Jerusalem* were led by the Angel of the Lord. The Angels of the Lord also directed *Daniel*. Therefore, there is nothing for anyone to boast about; *for it does not depend on the person who wills, nor on the one who runs, but on God who shows mercy* <note>original: Romans 9:16</note>, or on some other spiritual fate. All magic originates from this source and will return to it, whether it be good or evil. 

In this manner, *Tages* <note>A founding prophet of Etruscan religion who, according to legend, was plowed up from the earth as a child with the wisdom of an old man</note>, the first teacher of the magic of the Romans, emerged from the earth. *Diana of the Ephesians* revealed her own worship as if it had been sent from heaven; so also did *Apollo*. Indeed, all the religions of the heathens are taken from these same spirits; nor are the opinions of the *Sadducees* <note>A Jewish sect during the time of Christ that denied the existence of spirits, angels, and the resurrection</note> merely human inventions.

<summary>This page explains that spirits assist humans based on their moral nature and vocation, providing historical examples of spiritual influence. It asserts that all magic and religious revelation, whether divine or pagan, originate from the spiritual realm rather than human effort.</summary>
<keywords>vocation, spirits, Theodosius, Brutus, Hesiod, Muses, Homer, Ulysses, Hermes, Moses, Tages, Diana of the Ephesians, Sadducees, divine revelation</keywords>

# Arbatel of Magic

### Sciences

*   **Good**
    *   **Theosophy** <note>original: "Theoso-phy" — from the Greek *theos* (god) and *sophia* (wisdom); the study of divine things and the knowledge of God.</note>
        *   Knowledge of the Word of God, and ruling one's life according to the Word of God.
        *   Knowledge of the government of God by Angels, whom the Scripture calls **watchmen** <note>original: "watchmen" — a reference to the angelic "Watchers" mentioned in the biblical Book of Daniel.</note>; and to understand the mystery of Angels.
    *   **Anthroposophy** <note>original: "Anthro-sophy" — from the Greek *anthropos* (human) and *sophia* (wisdom); here referring to secular or natural human knowledge rather than the later 20th-century movement.</note> **given to man**
        *   Knowledge of natural things.
        *   Wisdom in human <note>original: "humane"</note> affairs.
*   **Evil**
    *   **Cacosophy** <note>original: "Cacoso-phy" — from the Greek *kakos* (bad/evil) and *sophia* (wisdom); the pursuit of wicked or false knowledge.</note>
        *   Contempt for the Word of God, and living according to the will of the devil.
        *   Ignorance of the government of God by Angels.
        *   To scorn the protection <note>original: "custody"</note> of the Angels, and choosing companions that are of the devil.
        *   Idolatry.
        *   Atheism.
    *   **Cacodaemony** <note>original: "Cacodæ-mony" — from the Greek *kakodaimonia*; the study of evil spirits or malevolent magic.</note>
        *   The knowledge of poisons in nature, and how to use them.
        *   Wisdom in all evil arts for the destruction of mankind, and to use them in contempt of God, and for the loss and destruction of men.

---

->THE END<-
<note>original: "FINIS"</note>
